&\ \ \leq C^{*}\bigg({\big\|\delta\tilde{p}^{(\rm e)}_{0}\big\|_{1,\alpha;\tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm in}}}+\big\|\delta z_{0}\big\|_{1,\alpha;\tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm h)}_{\rm in}}+\sum_{\rm k=\rm e,h}\big\|\delta\tilde{B}^{(\rm k)}_{0}\big\|_{1,\alpha;\tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm k)}_{\rm in}}+\sum_{\rm k=\rm e,h}\big\|\delta\tilde{S}^{(\rm k)}_{0}\big\|_{1,\alpha;\tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm k)}_{\rm in}}\\[5.0pt]
&\quad\qquad\ \ +{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\big\|\tilde{\omega}_{\rm e}\big\|^{(-1-\alpha,\{\mathcal{P}_{\rm e},\mathcal{Q}_{\rm e}\})}_{2,\alpha;\tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm ex}}+\big\|\omega_{\rm cd}\big\|^{(-\alpha,\{\mathcal{P}_{\rm e}\})}_{1,\alpha;\tilde{\Gamma}_{\rm cd}}}+\big\|g_{+}-1\big\|_{2,\alpha;\tilde{\Gamma}_{+}}+\big\|g_{-}+1\big\|_{2,\alpha;\tilde{\Gamma}_{-}}\bigg),\end{split}

(4.1)

where
C^{*}>0
depends only on
\tilde{\underline{U}}
and
L
. Here,
\delta\tilde{p}^{(\rm e)}_{0}=\tilde{p}^{(\rm e)}_{0}-\underline{p}^{(\rm e)}
and

\delta\tilde{B}^{(\rm k)}_{0}=\tilde{B}^{(\rm k)}_{0}-\underline{B}^{(\rm k)}
,
\delta\tilde{S}^{(\rm k)}_{0}=\tilde{S}^{(\rm k)}_{0}-\underline{S}^{(\rm k)}
for
\rm k=\rm e,h
.

We will show Theorem
4.1
by solving the problem
(\widetilde{\mathbf{FP}})_{\rm n}

first in
\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm e)}
and then in
\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h)}

together
.
To this end, we will first consider the problem
(\widetilde{\mathbf{FP}})_{\rm n}
for

\delta\varphi^{(\rm n)}
in the subsonic region
\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm e)}
, then we can deduce the boundary condition of
\delta z^{(\rm n)}
on
\tilde{\Gamma}_{\rm cd}
by gluing the solution
\delta\varphi^{(\rm n)}
obtained in

\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm e)}
. With this boundary condition for
\delta z^{(\rm n)}
on
\tilde{\Gamma}_{\rm cd}
, we can
solve the problem
(\widetilde{\mathbf{FP}})_{\rm n}
for
\delta z^{(\rm n)}
in the supersonic region
\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h)}

by employing the characteristics method.

4.1.
Estimates of solutions in the subsonic region
\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm e)}
for problem
(\widetilde{\mathbf{FP}})_{\rm n}

Let us consider the problem
(\widetilde{\mathbf{FP}})_{\rm n}
in the subsonic region
\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm e)}
(see Fig.
4.5
).
Problem
(\widetilde{\mathbf{FP}})_{\rm n}
in
\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm e)}
is the following boundary value problem for
\varphi^{(\rm n)}
:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{llll}\partial_{\xi}\mathcal{N}_{1}(D\varphi^{(\rm n)};\tilde{B}^{(\rm e)}_{0},\tilde{S}^{(\rm e)}_{0})+\partial_{\eta}\mathcal{N}_{2}(D\varphi^{(\rm n)};\tilde{B}^{(\rm e)}_{0},\tilde{S}^{(\rm e)}_{0})=0,&\ \ \ \mbox{in}\ \ \ \tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm e)},\\[5.0pt]
\delta\varphi^{(\rm n)}=g^{(\rm n)}_{0}(\eta),&\ \ \ \mbox{on}\ \ \ \tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm in},\\[5.0pt]
\partial_{\xi}\delta\varphi^{(\rm n)}=\tilde{\omega}_{\rm e}(\eta),&\ \ \ \mbox{on}\ \ \ \tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm ex},\\[5.0pt]
\delta\varphi^{(\rm n)}=g^{(\rm n)}_{+}(\xi),&\ \ \ \mbox{on}\ \ \ \tilde{\Gamma}_{+},\\[5.0pt]
\delta\varphi^{(\rm n)}=\int^{\xi}_{0}\omega_{\rm cd}(\nu)d\nu,&\ \ \ \mbox{on}\ \ \ \tilde{\Gamma}_{\rm cd},\end{array}\right.

where
\delta\varphi^{(n)}(0,0)=0
, and
\mathcal{N}_{j}\ (j=1,2)
,
c_{\rm cd}(\xi)
, and
g^{(\rm n)}_{0}(\eta)
,
g^{(\rm n)}_{+}(\eta)

are given in (
3.101
) and (
3.104
)-(
3.105
), respectively. Our main result in this section is the following proposition.

\tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm in}
\tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm ex}
\tilde{\Gamma}_{+}
\tilde{\Gamma}_{\rm cd}
\xi=0
\eta=m^{(\rm e)}
\eta=0
\xi=L
\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm e)}

Fig. 4.5.
Boundary value problem for
(\widetilde{\mathbf{FP}})_{\rm n}
in
\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm e)}

Proposition 4.1
.

For any given
m^{(\rm e)}\in\mathcal{M}_{\sigma}
with
\sigma>0
sufficiently small
,
there exist some constants
\alpha_{0}\in(0,1)
,
\mathcal{C}_{\rm e,0}>0
and
\varepsilon_{\rm e,0}>0
depending only on
\tilde{\underline{U}}
and
L
, such that for
\tilde{\epsilon}<\varepsilon_{\rm e}\in(\mathcal{C}_{\rm e,0}\tilde{\epsilon},\varepsilon_{\rm e,0})
with
\tilde{\epsilon}>0
sufficiently small,
\varrho\in(0,\mathcal{C}^{-1}_{\rm e,0}\varepsilon_{\rm e})
and
\alpha\in(0,\alpha_{0})
,
if
\omega_{\rm cd}\in\mathcal{W}
with
\|\omega_{\rm cd}\|^{(-\alpha,\{\mathcal{P}_{\rm e}\})}_{1,\alpha;\tilde{\Gamma}_{\rm cd}}\leq\varrho
,
then the problem (
4.1
) admits a unique solution
\varphi^{(\rm n)}\in{C^{2,\alpha}_{(-1-\alpha;\tilde{\Sigma}^{(\rm e)}\backslash\{\mathcal{O}\})}}\big(\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm e)}\big)

satisfying

\big\|\delta\varphi^{(\rm n)}\big\|^{(-1-\alpha,\tilde{\Sigma}^{(\rm e)}\backslash\{\mathcal{O}\})}_{2,\alpha;\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm e)}}\leq\varepsilon_{\rm e}
. Moreover, it holds that

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\big\|\delta\varphi^{(\rm n)}\big\|^{(-1-\alpha,\tilde{\Sigma}^{(\rm e)}\backslash\{\mathcal{O}\})}_{2,\alpha;\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm e)}}}&\leq C^{*}_{40}\bigg({\big\|\delta\tilde{p}^{(\rm e)}_{0}\big\|_{1,\alpha;\tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm in}}}+\big\|\delta\tilde{B}^{(\rm e)}_{0}\big\|_{1,\alpha;\tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm in}}+\big\|\delta\tilde{S}^{(\rm e)}_{0}\big\|_{1,\alpha;\tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm in}}\\[5.0pt]
&\qquad\quad\ \ +{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\big\|\tilde{\omega}_{\rm e}\big\|^{(-1-\alpha,\{\mathcal{P}_{\rm e},\mathcal{Q}_{\rm e}\})}_{2,\alpha;\tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm ex}}+\big\|\omega_{\rm cd}\big\|^{(-\alpha,\{\mathcal{P}_{\rm e}\})}_{1,\alpha;\tilde{\Gamma}_{\rm cd}}}+\big\|g_{+}-1\big\|_{2,\alpha;\tilde{\Gamma}_{+}}\bigg),\end{split}

(4.8)

where the constant
C^{*}_{40}>0
depends only on
\tilde{\underline{U}}
and
L
, and
\delta\tilde{p}^{(\rm e)}_{0}
,
\delta\tilde{B}^{(\rm e)}_{0}
,
\delta\tilde{S}^{(\rm e)}_{0}

are given by Theorem
4.1
.

Remark 4.1
.

From (
4.8
), it is easy to prove that there exists a constant
\tilde{C}^{*}_{40}>0

depending only on
\tilde{\underline{U}}
,
L
and
\alpha_{0}
such that

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\big\|\delta\varphi^{(\rm n)}\big\|^{(-1-\alpha,\{\mathcal{P}_{\rm e}\})}_{2,\alpha;\bar{\tilde{\Gamma}}_{\rm cd}}}&\leq\tilde{C}^{*}_{40}\bigg({\big\|\delta\tilde{p}^{(\rm e)}_{0}\big\|_{1,\alpha;\tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm in}}}+\big\|\delta\tilde{B}^{(\rm e)}_{0}\big\|_{1,\alpha;\tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm in}}+\big\|\delta\tilde{S}^{(\rm e)}_{0}\big\|_{1,\alpha;\tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm in}}\\[5.0pt]
&\qquad\quad\ \ +{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\big\|\tilde{\omega}_{\rm e}\big\|^{(-1-\alpha,\{\mathcal{P}_{\rm e},\mathcal{Q}_{\rm e}\})}_{2,\alpha;\tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm ex}}+\big\|\omega_{\rm cd}\big\|^{(-\alpha,\{\mathcal{P}_{\rm e}\})}_{1,\alpha;\tilde{\Gamma}_{\rm cd}}}+\big\|g_{+}-1\big\|_{2,\alpha;\tilde{\Gamma}_{+}}\bigg).\end{split}

(4.9)

Proof.

To prove Proposition
4.1
, we will first consider the existence and uniqueness of solutions to the problem (
4.1
) in the weight Hölder space due to the complexity of boundary conditions and the corner points of the domain. This can be achieved by developing a nonlinear iteration scheme near the background state together with the Banach fixed point argument.
Next, for the solution of the nonlinear problem, we will further show the
C^{2,\alpha}
-regularity of the solution
\varphi^{(\rm n)}
near the corner point
\mathcal{O}=(0,0)
to ensure that the solution
z^{(\rm n)}
in the supersonic region
\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h)}
is also solvable.
The proof is divided into three steps.

Step 1. Linearization of problem (
4.1
) in
\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm e)}
.
For fixed
\rm n
, define the iteration set

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\bf\Xi}_{\varepsilon_{\rm e}}=\big\{\varphi^{(\rm n)}:\ \|\varphi^{(\rm n)}-\underline{\varphi}\|^{(-1-\alpha,\tilde{\Sigma}^{(\rm e)})}_{2,\alpha;\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm e)}}\leq\varepsilon_{\rm e}\big\}.\end{split}

Let

\displaystyle\begin{split}\partial_{1}:=\partial_{\xi},\quad\partial_{2}:=\partial_{\eta}.\end{split}

(4.10)

Notice that the background state
\underline{\varphi}
satisfies

\displaystyle\begin{split}\partial_{1}\mathcal{N}_{1}(D\underline{\varphi};{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\underline{B}^{(\rm e)},\underline{S}^{(\rm e)}})+\partial_{2}\mathcal{N}_{2}(D\underline{\varphi};{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\underline{B}^{(\rm e)},\underline{S}^{(\rm e)}})=0.\end{split}

(4.11)

Taking the difference of equations
\eqref{eq:4.2}_{1}
and (
4.11
), and then linearizing the resulting equation, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sum_{i,j=1,2}\partial_{i}\big(a^{(\delta\varphi^{(\rm n)})}_{ij}\partial_{j}\delta\hat{\varphi}^{(\rm n)}\big)=\sum_{i=1,2}\partial_{i}b_{i},\end{split}

where

\displaystyle\begin{split}a^{(\delta\varphi^{(\rm n)})}_{ij}=\int^{1}_{0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\partial_{\partial\varphi^{(\rm n)}_{j}}}\mathcal{N}_{i}\big(D\underline{\varphi}+\tau D\delta\varphi^{(\rm n)};\tilde{B}^{(\rm e)}_{0},\tilde{S}^{(\rm e)}_{0}\big)d\tau,\ \ i,j=1,2,\end{split}

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}b_{i}=\mathcal{N}_{i}(D\underline{\varphi};\tilde{B}^{(\rm e)}_{0},\tilde{S}^{(\rm e)}_{0})-\mathcal{N}_{i}(D\underline{\varphi};\underline{B}^{(\rm e)},\underline{S}^{(\rm e)}),\ \ i=1,2.\end{split}

Therefore, we can derive the following linearized elliptic problem for the problem (
4.1
) in
\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm e)}
:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{llll}\sum_{i,j=1,2}\partial_{i}\big(a^{(\delta\varphi^{(\rm n)})}_{ij}\partial_{j}\delta\hat{\varphi}^{(\rm n)}\big)=\sum_{i=1,2}\partial_{i}b_{i},&\ \ \ \mbox{in}\ \ \ \tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm e)},\\[5.0pt]
\delta\hat{\varphi}^{(\rm n)}=g^{(\rm n)}_{0}(\eta),&\ \ \ \mbox{on}\ \ \ \tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm in},\\[5.0pt]
\partial_{1}\delta\hat{\varphi}^{(\rm n)}=\tilde{\omega}_{\rm e},&\ \ \ \mbox{on}\ \ \ \tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm ex},\\[5.0pt]
\delta\hat{\varphi}^{(\rm n)}=g^{(\rm n)}_{+}(\xi),&\ \ \ \mbox{on}\ \ \ \tilde{\Gamma}_{+},\\[5.0pt]
\delta\hat{\varphi}^{(\rm n)}=\int^{\xi}_{0}\omega_{\rm cd}(\nu)d\nu,&\ \ \ \mbox{on}\ \ \ \tilde{\Gamma}_{\rm cd},\end{array}\right.

where
g^{(\rm n)}_{0}
and
g^{(\rm n)}_{+}
are given by (
3.105
).

Define the map
\hat{\mathcal{T}}:\ \bf\Xi_{\varepsilon_{\rm e}}\longrightarrow\bf\Xi_{\varepsilon_{\rm e}}
as follows.
For a given function
{\varphi}^{(\rm n)}\in\bf\Xi_{\varepsilon_{\rm e}}
, we solve the linearized
fixed boundary value problem (
4.1
). Denote the solution by
\hat{\varphi}^{(\rm n)}
. Then

\displaystyle\delta\hat{\varphi}^{(n)}:=\hat{\mathcal{T}}(\delta{\varphi}^{(\rm n)}),\qquad\mbox{for}\quad\delta\hat{\varphi}^{(\rm n)}:=\hat{\varphi}^{(\rm n)}-\underline{\varphi}.

(4.18)

Step 2. Solve
\hat{\varphi}^{(n)}
by the fixed point argument.

For the coefficients
a^{(\delta\varphi^{(\rm n)})}_{ij}(i,j=1,2)
, it is easy to see that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\big\|a^{(\delta\varphi^{(\rm n)})}_{ij}-a^{(\delta\underline{\varphi})}_{ij}\big\|^{(-\alpha,\tilde{\Sigma}^{(\rm e)})}_{1,\alpha;\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm e)}}\leq\mathcal{C}\varepsilon_{\rm e},\end{split}

(4.19)

where
\mathcal{C}
is a positive constant depending only on
\underline{U}^{(\rm e)}
and
\alpha
.
Moreover, we have that

\displaystyle\begin{split}e_{1}:=a^{(\delta\underline{\varphi})}_{11}=\underline{u}^{(e)}>0,\ \ e_{2}:=a^{(\delta\underline{\varphi})}_{22}=\frac{\underline{\rho}^{(e)}(\underline{u}^{(e)})^{3}(\underline{c}^{(e)})^{2}}{(\underline{c}^{(e)})^{2}-(\underline{u}^{(e)})^{2}}>0,\end{split}

(4.20)

and

a^{(\delta\underline{\varphi})}_{12}=a^{(\delta\underline{\varphi})}_{21}=0
,
which yields

\displaystyle\begin{split}a^{(\delta\underline{\varphi})}_{11}a^{(\delta\underline{\varphi})}_{22}-a^{(\delta\underline{\varphi})}_{12}a^{(\delta\underline{\varphi})}_{21}=\frac{\underline{\rho}^{(e)}(\underline{u}^{(e)})^{4}(\underline{c}^{(e)})^{2}}{(\underline{c}^{(e)})^{2}-(\underline{u}^{(e)})^{2}}>0.\end{split}

(4.21)

If
\varepsilon_{\rm e}>0
is sufficiently small, there exist constants
\check{\lambda},\ \hat{\lambda}>0

depending only on
\underline{U}^{(e)}
, such that for any
\varphi^{(\rm n)}\in\bf\Xi_{\varepsilon_{\rm e}}
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\check{\lambda}|\mathbf{\vartheta}|\leq\sum_{i,j=1,2}a^{(\delta\varphi^{(\rm n)})}_{ij}\vartheta_{i}\vartheta_{j}\leq\hat{\lambda}|\mathbf{\vartheta}|,\ \ \ \mathbf{\vartheta}=(\vartheta_{1},\vartheta_{2}).\end{split}

(4.22)

From (
3.107
), at the background solution the coefficients
\beta^{(\rm n)}_{0,1}
and
\beta^{(\rm n)}_{0,2}
are

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left.\underline{\beta}_{0,1}=\beta^{(\rm n)}_{0,1}\right.\Big|_{\varphi^{(\rm n)}=\underline{\varphi},\tilde{B}^{(\rm e)}_{0}=\underline{B}^{(\rm e)},\tilde{S}^{(\rm e)}_{0}=\underline{S}^{(\rm e)}}=0,\\[5.0pt]
&\left.\underline{\beta}_{0,2}=\beta^{(\rm n)}_{0,2}\right.\Big|_{\varphi^{(\rm n)}=\underline{\varphi},\tilde{B}^{(\rm e)}_{0}=\underline{B}^{(\rm e)},\tilde{S}^{(\rm e)}_{0}=\underline{S}^{(\rm e)}}=\frac{(\underline{\rho}^{(e)}\underline{c}^{(e)})^{2}(\underline{u}^{(e)})^{3}}{(\underline{c}^{(e)})^{2}-(\underline{u}^{(e)})^{2}}>0.\end{split}

(4.23)

Notice that for sufficiently small constant
\sigma>0
defined by (
3.76
), one has
|\eta|\leq|m^{(\rm e)}|<\mathcal{C}
for
(0,\eta)\in\tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm in}
where the constant

\mathcal{C}>0
depends only on
\underline{U}^{(\rm e)}
. Then, we can deduce that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\big\|g^{(\rm n)}_{0}\big\|^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(-1-\alpha,\{\mathcal{O},\mathcal{Q}_{\rm I}\})}}_{2,\alpha;\tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm in}}\\[5.0pt]
&=\frac{1}{\underline{\beta}_{0,2}}\bigg\|\int^{\eta}_{0}\Big(\tilde{p}^{(\rm e)}_{0}(\mu)-\underline{\tilde{p}}^{(\rm e)}_{0}(\mu)-\beta^{\rm(n)}_{0,1}\partial_{1}\delta\varphi^{(\rm n)}-(\beta^{\rm(n)}_{0,2}-\underline{\beta}_{0,2})\partial_{2}\delta\varphi^{(\rm n)}\Big)d\mu\bigg\|^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(-1-\alpha,\{\mathcal{O},\mathcal{Q}_{\rm I}\})}}_{2,\alpha;\tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm in}}\\[5.0pt]
&\leq\mathcal{C}\bigg({\big\|\delta\tilde{p}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm 0}\big\|_{1,\alpha;\tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm in}}}+\big\|\delta\tilde{B}^{(\rm e)}_{0}\big\|_{1,\alpha;\tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm in}}+\big\|\delta\tilde{S}^{(\rm e)}_{0}\big\|_{1,\alpha;\tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm in}}\\[5.0pt]
&\ \ \ +\Big({\big\|\delta\varphi^{(\rm n)}\big\|^{(-1-\alpha,\tilde{\Sigma}^{(\rm e)})}_{2,\alpha;\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm e)}}}+\big\|\delta\tilde{B}^{(\rm e)}_{0}\big\|_{1,\alpha;\tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm in}}+\big\|\delta\tilde{S}^{(\rm e)}_{0}\big\|_{1,\alpha;\tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm in}}\Big){\big\|\delta\varphi^{(\rm n)}\big\|^{(-1-\alpha,\tilde{\Sigma}^{(\rm e)})}_{2,\alpha;\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm e)}}}\bigg),\end{split}

(4.24)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\big\|g^{(\rm n)}_{+}\big\|^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(-1-\alpha,\{\mathcal{Q}_{\rm I},\mathcal{Q}_{\rm e}\})}}_{2,\alpha;\tilde{\Gamma}_{+}}\\[5.0pt]
&=\big\|g_{+}(\xi)-g_{+}(0)+g^{(\rm n)}_{0}(m^{(\rm e)})\big\|^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(-1-\alpha,\{\mathcal{Q}_{\rm I},\mathcal{Q}_{\rm e}\})}}_{2,\alpha;\tilde{\Gamma}_{+}}\\[5.0pt]
&\leq\bigg\|\frac{1}{\underline{\beta}_{0,2}}\int^{m^{(\rm e)}}_{0}\Big(\tilde{p}^{(\rm e)}_{0}(\mu)-\underline{\tilde{p}}^{(\rm e)}_{0}(\mu)-\beta^{\rm(n)}_{0,1}\partial_{1}\delta\varphi^{(\rm n)}-(\beta^{\rm(n)}_{0,2}-\underline{\beta}_{0,2})\partial_{2}\delta\varphi^{(\rm n)}\Big)d\mu\bigg\|^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(-1-\alpha,\{\mathcal{Q}_{\rm I},\mathcal{Q}_{\rm e}\})}}_{2,\alpha;\tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm in}}\\[5.0pt]
&\ \ \ +\big\|g_{+}(\xi)-g_{+}(0)\big\|^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(-1-\alpha,\{\mathcal{Q}_{\rm I},\mathcal{Q}_{\rm e}\})}}_{2,\alpha;\tilde{\Gamma}_{+}}\\[5.0pt]
&\leq\mathcal{C}\bigg(\big\|g_{+}-1\big\|_{2,\alpha;\tilde{\Gamma}_{+}}+{\big\|\delta\tilde{p}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm 0}\big\|_{1,\alpha;\tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm in}}}+\big\|\delta\tilde{B}^{(\rm e)}_{0}\big\|_{1,\alpha;\tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm in}}+\big\|\delta\tilde{S}^{(\rm e)}_{0}\big\|_{1,\alpha;\tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm in}}\\[5.0pt]
&\ \ \ +\Big({\big\|\delta\varphi^{(\rm n)}\big\|^{(-1-\alpha,\tilde{\Sigma}^{(\rm e)})}_{2,\alpha;\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm e)}}}+\big\|\delta\tilde{B}^{(\rm e)}_{0}\big\|_{1,\alpha;\tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm in}}+\big\|\delta\tilde{S}^{(\rm e)}_{0}\big\|_{1,\alpha;\tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm in}}\Big){\big\|\delta\varphi^{(\rm n)}\big\|^{(-1-\alpha,\tilde{\Sigma}^{(\rm e)})}_{2,\alpha;\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm e)}}}\bigg),\end{split}

(4.25)

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}\big\|\delta\hat{\varphi}\big\|^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(-1-\alpha,\{\mathcal{O},\mathcal{P}_{\rm e}\})}}_{2,\alpha;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\tilde{\Gamma}_{\rm cd}}}=\bigg\|\int^{\xi}_{0}\omega_{\rm cd}(\nu)d\nu\bigg\|^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(-1-\alpha,\{\mathcal{O},\mathcal{P}_{\rm e}\})}}_{2,\alpha;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\tilde{\Gamma}_{\rm cd}}}\leq\mathcal{C}\big\|\omega_{\rm cd}\big\|^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(-\alpha,\{\mathcal{P}_{\rm e}\})}}_{1,\alpha;\tilde{\Gamma}_{\rm cd}},\end{split}

(4.26)

where
\mathcal{C}>0
depends only on
\underline{U}^{(\rm e)}
,
L
and
\alpha
,
and
\mathcal{Q}_{\rm I}=(0,m^{(\rm e)})
.

Now we can apply Theorem 2 in
[
38
]
and Theorem 3.2 in
[
39
]
as well as Lemma 6.29 in
[
35
]

to conclude that the problem (
4.1
) admits a
unique solution
\hat{\varphi}^{(\rm n)}\in{C^{2,\alpha}_{(-1-\alpha,\tilde{\Sigma}^{(\rm e)})}}\big(\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm e)}\big)
which satisfies

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\quad\ \big\|\delta\hat{\varphi}^{(\rm n)}\big\|^{(-1-\alpha,\tilde{\Sigma}^{(\rm e)})}_{2,\alpha;\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm e)}}\\[5.0pt]
&\leq\mathcal{C}\bigg(\big\|g^{(\rm n)}_{0}\big\|^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(-1-\alpha,\{\mathcal{O},\mathcal{Q}_{\rm I}\})}}_{2,\alpha;\tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm in}}+\big\|g^{(\rm n)}_{+}\big\|^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(-1-\alpha,\{\mathcal{Q}_{\rm I},\mathcal{Q}_{\rm e}\})}}_{2,\alpha;\tilde{\Gamma}_{+}}+\big\|\tilde{\omega}_{\rm e}\big\|^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(-1-\alpha,\{\mathcal{P}_{\rm e},\mathcal{Q}_{\rm e}\})}}_{2,\alpha;\tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm ex}}\\[5.0pt]
&\qquad\qquad\ +\sum_{i=1,2}\big\|b_{i}\big\|^{(-\alpha,\tilde{\Sigma}^{(\rm e)})}_{1,\alpha;\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm e)}}+\big\|\delta\hat{\varphi}\big\|^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(-1-\alpha,\{\mathcal{O},\mathcal{P}_{\rm e}\})}}_{2,\alpha;\tilde{\Gamma}_{\rm cd}}\bigg)\\[5.0pt]
&\leq\mathcal{C}\Big({\big\|\delta\varphi^{(\rm n)}\big\|^{(-1-\alpha,\tilde{\Sigma}^{(\rm e)})}_{2,\alpha;\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm e)}}}+\big\|\delta\tilde{B}^{(\rm e)}_{0}\big\|_{1,\alpha;\tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm in}}+\big\|\delta\tilde{S}^{(\rm e)}_{0}\big\|_{1,\alpha;\tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm in}}\Big)\big\|\delta\varphi^{(\rm n)}\big\|^{(-1-\alpha,\tilde{\Sigma}^{(\rm e)})}_{2,\alpha;\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm e)}}\\[5.0pt]
&\ \ \ \ +\mathcal{C}\bigg({\big\|\delta\tilde{p}^{(\rm e)}_{0}\big\|_{1,\alpha;\tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm in}}}+\big\|\delta\tilde{B}^{(\rm e)}_{0}\big\|_{1,\alpha;\tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm in}}+\big\|\delta\tilde{S}^{(\rm e)}_{0}\big\|_{1,\alpha;\tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm in}}+\big\|\tilde{\omega}_{\rm e}\big\|^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(-1-\alpha,\{\mathcal{P}_{\rm e},\mathcal{Q}_{\rm e}\})}}_{2,\alpha;\tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm ex}}\\[5.0pt]
&\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ +\big\|g_{+}-1\big\|_{2,\alpha;\tilde{\Gamma}_{+}}+{\big\|\omega_{\rm cd}\big\|^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(-\alpha,\{\mathcal{P}_{\rm e}\})}}_{1,\alpha;\tilde{\Gamma}_{\rm cd}}}\bigg),\end{split}

(4.27)

where
\mathcal{C}>0
depends only on
\underline{U}^{(\rm e)}
,
L
and
\alpha
.

Because
{\varphi}^{(\rm n)}\in\bf\Xi_{\varepsilon_{\rm e}}
, it follows from
Step 1
that
\hat{\varphi}^{(\rm n)}
is uniquely solved, then
\hat{\mathcal{T}}
is well-defined.
Moreover, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\big\|\delta\hat{\varphi}^{(\rm n)}\big\|^{(-1-\alpha,\tilde{\Sigma}^{(\rm e)})}_{2,\alpha;\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm e)}}\leq\mathcal{C}\varepsilon^{2}_{\rm e}+\mathcal{C}\tilde{\epsilon}\varepsilon_{\rm e}+\mathcal{C}(\tilde{\epsilon}+\varrho).}\end{split}

(4.28)

Choosing
\varepsilon^{\prime}_{\rm e,0}=\min\{\frac{1}{4\mathcal{C}},\frac{1}{2(\mathcal{C}+1)},1\}
,
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{\rm e,0}=4\mathcal{C}
and taking
\tilde{\epsilon}>0
sufficiently small with

\tilde{\epsilon}<\varepsilon_{\rm e}\in(\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{\rm e,0}\tilde{\epsilon},\varepsilon^{\prime}_{\rm e,0})
,
we see by (
4.28
) that
\hat{\varphi}^{(\rm n)}\in\bf\Xi_{\varepsilon_{\rm e}}
for
\varrho\in(0,\mathcal{C^{\prime}}_{\rm e,0}^{-1}\varepsilon_{\rm e})
, which means that
\hat{\mathcal{T}}
map
\bf\Xi_{\varepsilon_{\rm e}}
to
\bf\Xi_{\varepsilon_{\rm e}}
itself.

Next, we will show that
\hat{\mathcal{T}}
is a contraction map in
\bf\Xi_{\varepsilon_{\rm e}}
. Choose
\varphi^{(\rm n)}_{1},\varphi^{(\rm n)}_{2}\in\bf\Xi_{\varepsilon_{\rm e}}
.
Set
\delta\hat{\varphi}^{(\rm n)}_{k}:=\hat{\mathcal{T}}(\delta{\varphi}^{(\rm n)}_{k}),\ k=1,2
.
Then, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sum_{i,j=1,2}\partial_{i}\big(a^{(\delta\varphi^{(\rm n)}_{k})}_{ij}\partial_{j}\delta\hat{\varphi}^{(\rm n)}_{k}\big)=\sum_{i=1,2}\partial_{i}b_{i},\qquad\mbox{for}\quad k=1,2.\end{split}

(4.29)

Define

\delta\Phi^{(\rm n)}=\delta\varphi^{(\rm n)}_{1}-\delta\varphi^{(\rm n)}_{2},\quad\delta\hat{\Phi}^{(\rm n)}=\delta\hat{\varphi}^{(\rm n)}_{1}-\delta\hat{\varphi}^{(\rm n)}_{2}.

Then,
\delta\hat{\Phi}
satisfies the following boundary value problem:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{llll}&\sum_{i,j=1,2}\partial_{i}\big(a^{(\delta\varphi^{(\rm n)}_{1})}_{ij}\partial_{j}\delta\hat{\Phi}^{(\rm n)}\big)\\[5.0pt]
&\qquad\qquad\ =-\sum_{i,j=1,2}\partial_{i}\Big(\big(a^{(\delta\varphi^{(\rm n)}_{1})}_{ij}-a^{(\delta\varphi^{(\rm n)}_{2})}_{ij}\big)\partial_{j}\delta\hat{\varphi}^{(\rm n)}_{2}\Big),&\ \ \ \mbox{in}\ \ \ \tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm e)},\\[5.0pt]
&\delta\hat{\Phi}^{(\rm n)}=g^{(\rm n)}_{0,1}(\eta)-g^{(\rm n)}_{0,2}(\eta),&\ \ \ \mbox{on}\ \ \ \tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm in},\\[5.0pt]
&\partial_{1}\delta\hat{\Phi}^{(\rm n)}=0,&\ \ \ \mbox{on}\ \ \ \tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm ex},\\[5.0pt]
&\delta\hat{\Phi}^{(\rm n)}=g^{(\rm n)}_{+,1}(\xi)-g^{(\rm n)}_{+,2}(\xi),&\ \ \ \mbox{on}\ \ \ \tilde{\Gamma}_{+},\\[5.0pt]
&\delta\hat{\Phi}^{(\rm n)}=0,&\ \ \ \mbox{on}\ \ \ \tilde{\Gamma}_{\rm cd},\end{array}\right.

where
g^{(\rm n)}_{0,1}(\eta)
,
g^{(\rm n)}_{0,2}(\eta)
represent the right hand side of (
3.105
) by replacing
\varphi^{(\rm n)}

with
\varphi^{(\rm n)}_{1}
and
\varphi^{(\rm n)}_{2}
, respectively. Notice that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&g^{(\rm n)}_{0,1}(\eta)-g^{(\rm n)}_{0,2}(\eta)\\[5.0pt]
&=\frac{1}{\underline{\beta}_{0,2}}\int^{\eta}_{0}\bigg(\beta^{\rm(n)}_{0,1}|_{\varphi^{(\rm n)}=\varphi^{(\rm n)}_{1}}\partial_{1}\delta\varphi^{(\rm n)}_{1}+\Big(\beta^{\rm(n)}_{0,2}|_{\varphi^{(\rm n)}=\varphi^{(\rm n)}_{1}}-\underline{\beta}_{0,2}\Big)\partial_{2}\delta\varphi^{(\rm n)}_{1}-\beta^{\rm(n)}_{0,1}|_{\varphi^{(\rm n)}=\varphi^{(\rm n)}_{2}}\partial_{1}\delta\varphi^{(\rm n)}_{2}\\[5.0pt]
&\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ -\Big(\beta^{\rm(n)}_{0,2}|_{\varphi^{(\rm n)}=\varphi^{(\rm n)}_{2}}-\underline{\beta}_{0,2}\Big)\partial_{2}\delta\varphi^{(\rm n)}_{2}\bigg)d\mu\\[5.0pt]
&=\frac{1}{\underline{\beta}_{0,2}}\Bigg(\sum_{j=1,2}\int^{\eta}_{0}\Big(\beta^{\rm(n)}_{0,j}|_{\varphi^{(\rm n)}=\varphi^{(\rm n)}_{1}}-\underline{\beta}_{0,j}\Big)\partial_{j}\delta\Phi^{\rm(n)}d\mu+\int^{\eta}_{0}\partial_{j}\delta\varphi^{(\rm n)}_{2}\Big(\beta^{\rm(n)}_{0,j}|_{\varphi^{(\rm n)}=\varphi^{(\rm n)}_{1}}-\beta^{\rm(n)}_{0,j}|_{\varphi^{(\rm n)}=\varphi^{(\rm n)}_{2}}\Big)d\mu\Bigg).\end{split}

Then,
similarly to (
4.24
)-(
4.26
)
, we get that, for
\sigma>0
sufficiently small,

\displaystyle\big\|g^{(\rm n)}_{0,1}-g^{(\rm n)}_{0,2}\big\|^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(-1-\alpha,\{\mathcal{O},\mathcal{Q}_{\rm I}\})}}_{2,\alpha;\tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm in}}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \leq\frac{1}{\underline{\beta}_{0,2}}\bigg\|\sum_{j=1,2}\int^{\eta}_{0}\Big(\beta^{\rm(n)}_{0,j}|_{\varphi^{(\rm n)}=\varphi^{(\rm n)}_{1}}-\underline{\beta}_{0,j}\Big)\partial_{j}\delta\Phi^{\rm(n)}d\mu\bigg\|^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(-1-\alpha,\{\mathcal{O},\mathcal{Q}_{\rm I}\})}}_{2,\alpha;\tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm in}}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ +\frac{1}{\underline{\beta}_{0,2}}\bigg\|\sum_{j=1,2}\int^{\eta}_{0}\partial_{j}\delta\varphi^{(\rm n)}_{2}\Big(\beta^{\rm(n)}_{0,j}|_{\varphi^{(\rm n)}=\varphi^{(\rm n)}_{1}}-\beta^{\rm(n)}_{0,j}|_{\varphi^{(\rm n)}=\varphi^{(\rm n)}_{2}}\bigg)d\mu\Big\|^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(-1-\alpha,\{\mathcal{O},\mathcal{Q}_{\rm I}\})}}_{2,\alpha;\tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm in}}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \leq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{C}\big(\varepsilon_{\rm e}+\tilde{\epsilon}\big)}\big\|\delta\Phi^{(\rm n)}\big\|^{(-1-\alpha,\tilde{\Sigma}^{(\rm e)})}_{2,\alpha;\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm e)}},

where the constant
\mathcal{C}>0
depends only on
\underline{U}^{(\rm e)}
,
L
and
\alpha
.
In the same way, for
g^{(\rm n)}_{+,1}(\xi)-g^{(\rm n)}_{+,2}(\xi)
one also has

\displaystyle\big\|g^{(\rm n)}_{+,1}-g^{(\rm n)}_{+,2}\big\|^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(-1-\alpha,\{\mathcal{Q}_{\rm I},\mathcal{Q}_{\rm e}\})}}_{2,\alpha;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\tilde{\Gamma}_{+}}}\leq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{C}\big(\varepsilon_{\rm e}+\tilde{\epsilon}\big)}\big\|\delta\Phi^{(\rm n)}\big\|^{(-1-\alpha,\tilde{\Sigma}^{(\rm e)})}_{2,\alpha;\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm e)}},

provided that
\sigma>0
is sufficiently small, where the constant
\mathcal{C}>0
depends only on
\underline{U}^{(\rm e)}
,
L
and
\alpha
.

Thus, it follows from the estimate (
4.27
) that

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\big\|\delta\hat{\Phi}^{(\rm n)}\big\|^{(-1-\alpha,\tilde{\Sigma}^{(\rm e)})}_{2,\alpha;\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm e)}}}&\leq\mathcal{C}\bigg(\sum_{i,j=1,2}\big\|\big(a^{(\delta\varphi^{(\rm n)}_{1})}_{ij}-a^{(\delta\varphi^{(\rm n)}_{2})}_{ij}\big)\partial_{j}\delta\hat{\varphi}^{(\rm n)}_{2}\big\|^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(-\alpha;\tilde{\Sigma}^{(\rm e)})}}_{1,\alpha;\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm e)}}\bigg)\\[5.0pt]
&\ \ \ +\mathcal{C}\bigg(\big\|g^{(\rm n)}_{0,1}-g^{(\rm n)}_{0,2}\big\|^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(-1-\alpha,\{\mathcal{O},\mathcal{Q}_{\rm I}\})}}_{2,\alpha;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm in}}}+\big\|g^{(\rm n)}_{+,1}-g^{(\rm n)}_{+,2}\big\|^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(-1-\alpha,\{\mathcal{Q}_{\rm I},\mathcal{Q}_{\rm e}\})}}_{2,\alpha;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\tilde{\Gamma}_{+}}}\bigg)\\[5.0pt]
&\leq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{C}\big(\varepsilon_{\rm e}+\tilde{\epsilon}\big)}\big\|\delta\Phi^{(\rm n)}\big\|^{(-1-\alpha,\tilde{\Sigma}^{(\rm e)})}_{2,\alpha;\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm e)}},\end{split}

where
\mathcal{C}>0
depends only on
\underline{U}^{(\rm e)}
,
L
and
\alpha
.

Therefore, we show that
\mathcal{T}
is a contraction map by letting
\tilde{\epsilon}>0
sufficiently small so that

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\tilde{\epsilon}}<\varepsilon_{\rm e}\in(\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}_{\rm e,0}\tilde{\epsilon},\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}_{\rm e,0})
with

\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}_{\rm e,0}=1
and
\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}_{\rm e,0}=\frac{1}{4\mathcal{C}}
.
As a result, the existence of solutions to the problem (
4.1
) and the estimate (
4.8
) are established by (
4.27
)
for
\varepsilon_{\rm e}\in(\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}_{\rm e,0}\tilde{\epsilon},\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}_{\rm e,0})
.

For the uniqueness, consider two solutions
\varphi^{(\rm n)}_{1},\ \varphi^{(\rm n)}_{2}\in\bf\Xi_{\varepsilon_{\rm e}}

for any
\varepsilon_{\rm e}\in(\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}_{\rm e,0}\tilde{\epsilon},\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}_{\rm e,0})
and fixed
\rm n
. Notice that
\hat{\mathcal{T}}
is a contraction map, then we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\big\|\varphi^{(\rm n)}_{1}-\varphi^{(\rm n)}_{2}\big\|^{(-1-\alpha,\tilde{\Sigma}^{(\rm e)})}_{2,\alpha;\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm e)}}}\leq\frac{1}{2}{\big\|\varphi^{(\rm n)}_{1}-\varphi^{(\rm n)}_{2}\big\|^{(-1-\alpha,\tilde{\Sigma}^{(\rm e)})}_{2,\alpha;\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm e)}}},\end{split}

which implies that
\varphi^{(\rm n)}_{1}=\varphi^{(\rm n)}_{2}
.

Finally, we take
\mathcal{C}_{\rm e,0}=\max\{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{\rm e,0},\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}_{\rm e,0}\}
and
\varepsilon_{\rm e,0}=\min\{\varepsilon^{\prime}_{\rm e,0},\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}_{\rm e,0}\}
,
then, for
\tilde{\epsilon}>0
sufficiently small, if
\tilde{\epsilon}<\varepsilon_{\rm e}\in(\mathcal{C}_{\rm e,0}\tilde{\epsilon},\varepsilon_{\rm e,0})
and
\varrho\in(0,\mathcal{C}^{-1}_{\rm e,0}\varepsilon_{\rm e,0})
,
the problem (
4.1
) admits a unique solution
\varphi^{(\rm n)}\in{C^{2,\alpha}_{(-1-\alpha;\tilde{\Sigma}^{(\rm e)})}}\big(\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm e)}\big)

satisfying
\big\|\delta\varphi^{(\rm n)}\big\|^{(-1-\alpha,\tilde{\Sigma}^{(\rm e)})}_{2,\alpha;\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm e)}}\leq\varepsilon_{\rm e}
and the estimate (
4.27
).

Step 3. Higher order regularity of the solution near the corner point
\mathcal{O}
.
We divide this step into two sub-steps.

(Step 3.1)
Introduce a new function and reformulate the problem (
4.1
) near the corner point
\mathcal{O}
.
Set

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi^{(\rm n)}=\partial_{1}\varphi^{(\rm n)}.}

(4.37)

Then it is easy to see that

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\partial^{2}_{11}\varphi^{(\rm n)}=\partial_{1}\psi^{(\rm n)},\quad\partial^{2}_{12}\varphi^{(\rm n)}=\partial_{2}\psi^{(\rm n)}.}\end{split}

(4.38)

By the equation
\eqref{eq:4.2}_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sum_{i,j=1,2}\mathcal{N}^{(\rm n)}_{ij}\partial^{2}_{ij}\varphi^{(\rm n)}=-\partial_{\tilde{B}^{(\rm e)}_{0}}\mathcal{N}_{2}\cdot(\tilde{B}^{(e)}_{0})^{\prime}-\partial_{\tilde{S}^{(\rm e)}_{0}}\mathcal{N}_{2}\cdot(\tilde{S}^{(\rm e)}_{0})^{\prime},\end{split}

(4.39)

which gives

\displaystyle\begin{split}\partial^{2}_{22}\varphi^{(\rm n)}&=-\frac{\mathcal{N}^{(\rm n)}_{11}}{\mathcal{N}^{(\rm n)}_{22}}\partial^{2}_{11}\varphi^{(\rm n)}-\frac{2\mathcal{N}^{(\rm n)}_{12}}{\mathcal{N}^{(\rm n)}_{22}}\partial^{2}_{12}\varphi^{(\rm n)}-\frac{\partial_{\tilde{B}^{(\rm e)}_{0}}\mathcal{N}_{2}\cdot(\tilde{B}^{(e)}_{0})^{\prime}+\partial_{\tilde{S}^{(\rm e)}_{0}}\mathcal{N}_{2}\cdot(\tilde{S}^{(\rm e)}_{0})^{\prime}}{\mathcal{N}^{(\rm n)}_{22}}\\[5.0pt]
&=-\frac{\mathcal{N}^{(\rm n)}_{11}}{\mathcal{N}^{(\rm n)}_{22}}\partial_{1}\psi^{(\rm n)}-\frac{2\mathcal{N}^{(\rm n)}_{12}}{\mathcal{N}^{(\rm n)}_{22}}\partial_{2}\psi^{(\rm n)}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\frac{\partial_{\tilde{B}^{(\rm e)}_{0}}\mathcal{N}_{2}\cdot(\tilde{B}^{(e)}_{0})^{\prime}+\partial_{\tilde{S}^{(\rm e)}_{0}}\mathcal{N}_{2}\cdot(\tilde{S}^{(\rm e)}_{0})^{\prime}}{\mathcal{N}^{(\rm n)}_{22}},}\end{split}

(4.40)

where

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathcal{N}^{(\rm n)}_{11}=\partial_{\partial_{1}\varphi^{(\rm n)}}\mathcal{N}_{1}\big(D\varphi^{(\rm n)};\tilde{B}^{(\rm e)}_{0},\tilde{S}^{(\rm e)}_{0}\big)=\frac{(\tilde{\rho}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm n})^{2}(\tilde{c}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm n})^{2}(\partial_{2}\varphi^{(\rm n)})^{2}-1}{(\tilde{\rho}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm n})^{3}(\partial_{2}\varphi^{(\rm n)})^{3}\Big((\tilde{c}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm n})^{2}-\frac{1+(\partial_{1}\varphi^{(\rm n)})^{2}}{(\tilde{\rho}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm n})^{2}(\partial_{2}\varphi^{(\rm n)})^{2}}\Big)},\\[5.0pt]
&\mathcal{N}^{(\rm n)}_{22}=\partial_{\partial_{2}\varphi^{(\rm n)}}\mathcal{N}_{2}\big(D\varphi^{(\rm n)};\tilde{B}^{(\rm e)}_{0},\tilde{S}^{(\rm e)}_{0}\big)=\frac{(\tilde{c}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm n})^{2}\big((\partial_{1}\varphi^{(\rm n)})^{2}+1\big)}{\tilde{\rho}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm n}(\partial_{2}\varphi^{(\rm n)})^{3}\Big((\tilde{c}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm n})^{2}-\frac{1+(\partial_{1}\varphi^{(\rm n)})^{2}}{(\tilde{\rho}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm n})^{2}(\partial_{2}\varphi^{(\rm n)})^{2}}\Big)},\end{split}

(4.41)

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{N}^{(\rm n)}_{12}=\mathcal{N}^{(\rm n)}_{21}&=\partial_{\partial_{2}\varphi^{(\rm n)}}\mathcal{N}_{1}\big(D\varphi^{(\rm n)};\tilde{B}^{(\rm e)}_{0},\tilde{S}^{(\rm e)}_{0}\big)=-\frac{(\tilde{c}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm n})^{2}\partial_{1}\varphi^{(\rm n)}}{\tilde{\rho}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm n}(\partial_{2}\varphi^{(\rm n)})^{2}\Big((\tilde{c}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm n})^{2}-\frac{1+(\partial_{1}\varphi^{(\rm n)})^{2}}{(\tilde{\rho}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm n})^{2}(\partial_{2}\varphi^{(\rm n)})^{2}}\Big)}.\end{split}

(4.42)

Obviously,
\mathcal{N}^{(\rm n)}_{ij}
satisfies (
4.19
)–(
4.22
), and

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{N}^{(\rm n)}_{11}\mathcal{N}^{(\rm n)}_{22}-\mathcal{N}^{(\rm n)}_{12}\mathcal{N}^{(\rm n)}_{21}&=\frac{(\tilde{c}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm n})^{2}}{(\tilde{\rho}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm n})^{2}(\partial_{2}\varphi^{(\rm n)})^{4}\Big((\tilde{c}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm n})^{2}-\frac{1+(\partial_{1}\varphi^{(\rm n)})^{2}}{(\tilde{\rho}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm n})^{2}(\partial_{2}\varphi^{(\rm n)})^{2}}\Big)}\\[5.0pt]
&=\frac{(\tilde{c}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm n})^{2}(\tilde{\rho}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm n})^{2}(\tilde{u}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm n})^{2}}{(\tilde{c}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm n})^{2}-(\tilde{q}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm n})^{2}}>0,\end{split}

(4.43)

for any
\tilde{\rho}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm n}\tilde{u}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm n}>0
in
\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm e)}
,
where
\tilde{\rho}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm n},\tilde{c}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm n},\tilde{u}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm n}

and
\tilde{q}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm n}
are functions of
D\varphi^{(\rm n)},\tilde{B}^{(\rm e)}_{0},\tilde{S}^{(\rm e)}_{0}
.
Moreover, we know that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{N}^{(\rm n)}_{11}\big|_{\varphi^{(\rm n)}=\underline{\varphi},\tilde{B}^{(\rm e)}_{0}=\underline{B}^{(\rm e)},\tilde{S}^{(\rm e)}_{0}=\underline{S}^{(\rm e)}}>0,\ \ \ \mathcal{N}^{(\rm n)}_{22}\big|_{\varphi^{(\rm n)}=\underline{\varphi},\tilde{B}^{(\rm e)}_{0}=\underline{B}^{(\rm e)},\tilde{S}^{(\rm e)}_{0}=\underline{S}^{(\rm e)}}>0,\end{split}

(4.44)

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{N}^{(\rm n)}_{12}\big|_{\varphi^{(\rm n)}=\underline{\varphi},\tilde{B}^{(\rm e)}_{0}=\underline{B}^{(\rm e)},\tilde{S}^{(\rm e)}_{0}=\underline{S}^{(\rm e)}}=\mathcal{N}^{(\rm n)}_{21}\big|_{\varphi^{(\rm n)}=\underline{\varphi},\tilde{B}^{(\rm e)}_{0}=\underline{B}^{(\rm e)},\tilde{S}^{(\rm e)}_{0}=\underline{S}^{(\rm e)}}=0.\end{split}

(4.45)

In order to derive the equation for
\psi^{(\rm n)}
, we take
\partial_{1}
on (
4.39
)
and then substitute (
4.38
) into the resulting equation to obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sum_{i,j=1,2}\mathcal{N}^{(\rm n)}_{ij}\partial^{2}_{ij}\psi^{(\rm n)}+\sum_{k,j=1,2}b^{(\rm n)}_{kj}\partial_{k}\psi^{(\rm n)}\partial_{j}\psi^{(\rm n)}=f^{(\rm n)},\end{split}

(4.46)

where
\mathcal{N}^{(\rm n)}_{ij}\ (i,j=1,2)
are given by (
4.41
)–(
4.42
) with (
4.43
), and

\displaystyle\begin{split}&b^{(\rm n)}_{k1}=\partial_{\partial_{k}\varphi^{(\rm n)}}\mathcal{N}^{(\rm n)}_{11}-\frac{\mathcal{N}^{(\rm n)}_{11}}{\mathcal{N}^{(\rm n)}_{22}}\partial_{\partial_{k}\varphi^{(\rm n)}}\mathcal{N}^{(\rm n)}_{22},\\[5.0pt]
&b^{(\rm n)}_{k2}=2\Big(\partial_{\partial_{k}\varphi^{(\rm n)}}\mathcal{N}^{(\rm n)}_{12}-\frac{\mathcal{N}^{(\rm n)}_{12}}{\mathcal{N}^{(\rm n)}_{22}}\partial_{\partial_{k}\varphi^{(\rm n)}}\mathcal{N}^{(\rm n)}_{12}\Big),\end{split}

for
k=1,2

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}f^{(\rm n)}&=\sum_{i,j=1,2}\Big(\frac{\partial_{\partial_{k}\varphi^{(\rm n)}}\mathcal{N}^{(\rm n)}_{22}\partial_{\tilde{B}^{(\rm e)}_{0}}\mathcal{N}_{2}}{\mathcal{N}^{(\rm n)}_{22}}-\partial^{2}_{\tilde{B}^{(\rm e)}_{0}\partial_{k}\varphi^{(\rm n)}}\mathcal{N}_{2}\Big)\partial^{2}_{k1}\varphi^{(\rm n)}\cdot(\tilde{B}^{(e)}_{0})^{\prime}\\[5.0pt]
&\quad+\sum_{i,j=1,2}\Big(\frac{\partial_{\partial_{k}\varphi^{(\rm n)}}\mathcal{N}^{(\rm n)}_{22}\partial_{\tilde{S}^{(\rm e)}_{0}}\mathcal{N}_{2}}{\mathcal{N}^{(\rm n)}_{22}}-\partial^{2}_{\tilde{S}^{(\rm e)}_{0}\partial_{k}\varphi^{(\rm n)}}\mathcal{N}_{2}\Big)\partial^{2}_{k1}\varphi^{(\rm n)}\cdot(\tilde{S}^{(e)}_{0})^{\prime}.\end{split}

(4.47)

Next, let us consider the boundary conditions of
\psi^{(\rm n)}
. Firstly, at the entrance
\tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm in}
,
we rewrite
\eqref{eq:4.2}_{2}
as

\displaystyle\begin{split}\tilde{p}^{(\rm e)}(D\varphi^{(\rm n)},\tilde{B}^{(e)}_{0},\tilde{S}^{(e)}_{0})=\tilde{p}^{(\rm e)}_{0}(\eta).\end{split}

(4.48)

Then, taking derivative
\partial_{2}
on (
4.48
) and by (
4.37
) and (
4.40
) to deduce that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\tilde{\beta}^{(\rm n)}_{0,1}\partial_{1}\psi^{(\rm n)}+\tilde{\beta}^{(\rm n)}_{0,2}\partial_{2}\psi^{(\rm n)}=g^{(\rm n)},&\ \ \ \mbox{on}\ \ \ {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm in}},\end{split}

(4.49)

where

\displaystyle\begin{split}\tilde{\beta}^{(\rm n)}_{0,1}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-\frac{\partial_{\partial_{2}\varphi^{(\rm n)}}\tilde{p}^{(\rm e)}\mathcal{N}^{(\rm n)}_{11}}{\mathcal{N}^{(\rm n)}_{22}}},\qquad\tilde{\beta}^{(\rm n)}_{0,2}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\partial_{\partial_{1}\varphi^{(\rm n)}}\tilde{p}^{(\rm e)}-\frac{2\partial_{\partial_{2}\varphi^{(\rm n)}}\tilde{p}^{(\rm e)}\mathcal{N}^{(\rm n)}_{12}}{\mathcal{N}^{(\rm n)}_{22}}},\end{split}

(4.50)

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}g^{(\rm n)}&=(\tilde{p}^{(\rm e)}_{0})^{\prime}+\Big(\frac{\partial_{\partial_{2}\varphi^{(\rm n)}}\tilde{p}^{(\rm e)}\partial_{\tilde{B}^{(\rm e)}_{0}}\mathcal{N}_{2}}{\mathcal{N}^{(\rm n)}_{22}}-\partial_{\tilde{B}^{(\rm e)}_{0}}\tilde{p}^{(\rm e)}\Big)(\tilde{B}^{(\rm e)}_{0})^{\prime}\\[5.0pt]
&\qquad+\Big(\frac{\partial_{\partial_{2}\varphi^{(\rm n)}}\tilde{p}^{(\rm e)}\partial_{\tilde{S}^{(\rm e)}_{0}}\mathcal{N}_{2}}{\mathcal{N}^{(\rm n)}_{22}}-\partial_{\tilde{S}^{(\rm e)}_{0}}\tilde{p}^{(\rm e)}\Big)(\tilde{S}^{(\rm e)}_{0})^{\prime}.\end{split}

(4.51)

Here

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\partial_{\partial_{1}\varphi^{(\rm n)}}\tilde{p}^{(\rm e)}=-\frac{(\tilde{c}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm n})^{2}\partial_{1}\varphi^{(\rm n)}}{\tilde{\rho}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm n}(\partial_{2}\varphi^{(\rm n)})^{2}\Big((\tilde{c}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm n})^{2}-\frac{1+(\partial_{1}\varphi^{(\rm n)})^{2}}{(\tilde{\rho}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm n})^{2}(\partial_{2}\varphi^{(\rm n)})^{2}}\Big)},\\[5.0pt]
&\partial_{\partial_{2}\varphi^{(\rm n)}}\tilde{p}^{(\rm e)}=\frac{(\tilde{c}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm n})^{2}\big(1+(\partial_{1}\varphi^{(\rm n)})^{2}\big)}{\tilde{\rho}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm n}(\partial_{2}\varphi^{(\rm n)})^{3}\Big((\tilde{c}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm n})^{2}-\frac{1+(\partial_{1}\varphi^{(\rm n)})^{2}}{(\tilde{\rho}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm n})^{2}(\partial_{2}\varphi^{(\rm n)})^{2}}\Big)}.\end{split}

(4.52)

Furthermore, from the construction of the approximate solutions, and by Proposition
3.1
, (
3.1
), (
4.44
), (
4.45
),
(
4.50
) and (
4.52
), we know that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\tilde{\beta}^{(\rm n)}_{0,1}\big|_{\varphi^{(\rm n)}=\underline{\varphi},\tilde{B}^{(\rm e)}_{0}=\underline{B}^{(\rm e)},\tilde{S}^{(\rm e)}_{0}=\underline{S}^{(\rm e)}}\\[5.0pt]
&\ \ \ ={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-\frac{\partial_{\partial_{2}\varphi^{(\rm n)}}\tilde{p}^{(\rm e)}\big|_{\varphi^{(\rm n)}=\underline{\varphi},\tilde{B}^{(\rm e)}_{0}=\underline{B}^{(\rm e)},\tilde{S}^{(\rm e)}_{0}=\underline{S}^{(\rm e)}}\mathcal{N}^{(\rm n)}_{11}\big|_{\varphi^{(\rm n)}=\underline{\varphi},\tilde{B}^{(\rm e)}_{0}=\underline{B}^{(\rm e)},\tilde{S}^{(\rm e)}_{0}=\underline{S}^{(\rm e)}}}{\mathcal{N}^{(\rm n)}_{22}\big|_{\varphi^{(\rm n)}=\underline{\varphi},\tilde{B}^{(\rm e)}_{0}=\underline{B}^{(\rm e)},\tilde{S}^{(\rm e)}_{0}=\underline{S}^{(\rm e)}}}<0},\\[5.0pt]
&\tilde{\beta}^{(\rm n)}_{0,2}\big|_{\varphi^{(\rm n)}=\underline{\varphi},\tilde{B}^{(\rm e)}_{0}=\underline{B}^{(\rm e)},\tilde{S}^{(\rm e)}_{0}=\underline{S}^{(\rm e)}}\\[5.0pt]
&\ \ \ ={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\partial_{\partial_{1}\varphi^{(\rm n)}}\tilde{p}^{(\rm e)}\big|_{\varphi^{(\rm n)}=\underline{\varphi},\tilde{B}^{(\rm e)}_{0}=\underline{B}^{(\rm e)},\tilde{S}^{(\rm e)}_{0}=\underline{S}^{(\rm e)}}}\\[5.0pt]
&\ \ \ \ \ \ {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-\frac{2\partial_{\partial_{2}\varphi^{(\rm n)}}\tilde{p}^{(\rm e)}\big|_{\varphi^{(\rm n)}=\underline{\varphi},\tilde{B}^{(\rm e)}_{0}=\underline{B}^{(\rm e)},\tilde{S}^{(\rm e)}_{0}=\underline{S}^{(\rm e)}}\mathcal{N}^{(\rm n)}_{12}\big|_{\varphi^{(\rm n)}=\underline{\varphi},\tilde{B}^{(\rm e)}_{0}=\underline{B}^{(\rm e)},\tilde{S}^{(\rm e)}_{0}=\underline{S}^{(\rm e)}}}{\mathcal{N}^{(\rm n)}_{22}\big|_{\varphi^{(\rm n)}=\underline{\varphi},\tilde{B}^{(\rm e)}_{0}=\underline{B}^{(\rm e)},\tilde{S}^{(\rm e)}_{0}=\underline{S}^{(\rm e)}}}}\\[5.0pt]
&\ \ \ =0,\end{split}

(4.53)

where
\partial_{\partial_{j}\varphi^{(\rm n)}}\tilde{p}^{(\rm e)}
for
j=1,2
are given by (
4.52
).

Since
\partial_{1}\delta\varphi^{(\rm n)}=\omega_{\rm cd}
on
\tilde{\Gamma}_{\rm cd}
, then we have, on the contact discontinuity,

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi^{(\rm n)}=\omega_{\rm cd}(\xi)},\ \ \ \ \mbox{on}\ \ \ \ \tilde{\Gamma}_{\rm cd}.\end{split}

(4.54)

(Step 3.2)
Construct a comparison function and apply the maximal principle near the corner point
\mathcal{O}
.
Let us introduce a coordinate transformation,

\displaystyle\hat{\pi}:\ \ \left\{\begin{array}[]{llll}\hat{\xi}=\sqrt{e_{1}}\xi,\\[5.0pt]
\hat{\eta}=\sqrt{e_{2}}\eta,\end{array}\right.

where
e_{1}
,
e_{2}
are given as in (
4.20
). Then under the transformation
\hat{\pi}
, the domain
\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm e)}
and boundaries
\tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm in}
and
\tilde{\Gamma}_{\rm cd}
become

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\hat{\Omega}^{(\rm e)}=\big\{(\hat{\xi},\hat{\eta}):\ 0<\hat{\xi}<\sqrt{e_{1}}L,\ 0<\hat{\eta}<\sqrt{e_{2}}m^{(\rm e)}\big\},\\[5.0pt]
&\hat{\Gamma}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm in}=\big\{(\hat{\xi},\hat{\eta}):\ \hat{\xi}=0,\ 0<\hat{\eta}<\sqrt{e_{2}}m^{(\rm e)}\big\},\\[5.0pt]
&\hat{\Gamma}_{\rm cd}=\big\{(\hat{\xi},\hat{\eta}):\ 0<\hat{\xi}<\sqrt{e_{1}}L,\ \hat{\eta}=0\big\}.\end{split}

Let

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathscr{L}^{(\rm n)}:=\sum_{i,j=1,2}\hat{\mathcal{N}}^{(\rm n)}_{ij}\hat{\partial}^{2}_{ij}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+\sum_{k,j=1,2}\hat{b}^{(\rm n)}_{kj}\hat{\partial}_{k}\hat{\partial}_{j}},\ \ \ \ \mathscr{M}^{(\rm n)}:=\hat{\beta}^{(\rm n)}_{0,1}\hat{\partial}_{1}+\hat{\beta}^{(\rm n)}_{0,2}\hat{\partial}_{2},\end{split}

(4.56)

where
\hat{\partial}_{1}:=\partial_{\hat{\xi}},\ \hat{\partial}_{2}:=\partial_{\hat{\eta}}
and

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\hat{\mathcal{N}}^{(\rm n)}_{ij}=\frac{\mathcal{N}^{(\rm n)}_{ij}\big(\hat{\pi}^{-1}(\hat{\xi},\hat{\eta})\big)}{\sqrt{e_{i}e_{j}}},\ \ {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\hat{b}^{(\rm n)}_{kj}=\frac{b^{(\rm n)}_{kj}\big(\hat{\pi}^{-1}(\hat{\xi},\hat{\eta})\big)}{\sqrt{e_{k}e_{j}}}},\ \ \hat{\beta}^{(\rm n)}_{0,j}=\frac{\tilde{\beta}^{(\rm n)}_{0,j}\big(\hat{\pi}^{-1}(\hat{\xi},\hat{\eta})\big)}{\sqrt{e_{j}}},\ \ \ i,k,j=1,2,\end{split}

(4.57)

satisfying (
4.44
), (
4.45
) and (
4.53
).
Set
\hat{\psi}^{(\rm n)}(\hat{\xi},\hat{\eta}):=\psi^{(\rm n)}\big(\hat{\pi}^{-1}(\hat{\xi},\hat{\eta})\big)
.
Then
\hat{\psi}^{(n)}
in the new coordinates
(\hat{\xi},\hat{\eta})
satisfies the following problem:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{llll}\mathscr{L}^{(\rm n)}(\hat{\psi}^{(\rm n)})=\hat{f}^{(\rm n)},&\qquad\ \ \ \mbox{in}\quad\hat{\Omega}^{(\rm e)},\\[5.0pt]
\mathscr{M}^{(\rm n)}(\hat{\psi}^{(\rm n)})=\hat{g}^{(\rm n)},&\qquad\ \ \ \mbox{on}\quad\hat{\Gamma}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm in},\\[5.0pt]
\hat{\psi}^{(\rm n)}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\omega_{\rm cd}(\hat{\xi}/\sqrt{e_{1}})},&\qquad\ \ \ \mbox{on}\quad\hat{\Gamma}_{\rm cd},\end{array}\right.

where
\hat{f}^{(\rm n)}
and
\hat{g}^{(\rm n)}
are functions
of
f^{(\rm n)}
and
{g}^{(\rm n)}
under the coordinate transformation of
\hat{\pi}
.

Note that due to the already obtained
C^{1,\alpha}
regularity of
\varphi^{(\rm n)}
up to the origin, one has the
C^{0,\alpha}
regularity of
\hat{\psi}^{(\rm n)}
,
hence the Dirichlet condition for
\hat{\psi}^{(\rm n)}
on
\hat{\Gamma}_{\rm cd}
can be extended to the origin.

We fix a constant
r_{0}>0
and let
B_{r_{0}}(\mathcal{O})
be a disk with center at
\mathcal{O}
and radius
r_{0}
. Denote
B^{+}_{r_{0}}=B_{r_{0}}(\mathcal{O})\cap\hat{\Omega}^{(\rm e)}
.
Let
r=\sqrt{\hat{\xi}^{2}+\hat{\eta}^{2}}
, and
\theta=\arctan\frac{\hat{\eta}}{\hat{\xi}}
.
Define

\displaystyle{\upsilon}(r,\theta)=Kr^{1+\alpha}\sin\big(\tau\theta+\omega\big),\quad(0\leq\theta\leq\frac{\pi}{2}),

(4.62)

where
\alpha\in(0,1)
,
\tau=\frac{3+\alpha}{2},\ \omega=\frac{1-\alpha}{8}\pi
and
K=\hat{K}r^{-1-\alpha}_{0}\varepsilon_{\rm e}

for
\hat{K}>0
being a constant to be determined later.
It follows from the direct computation that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\hat{\partial}_{1}\upsilon={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Kr^{\alpha}\big[(1+\alpha)\sin\big(\tau\theta+\omega\big)\cos\theta-\tau\cos\big(\tau\theta+\omega\big)\sin\theta\big]},\\[5.0pt]
&\hat{\partial}_{2}\upsilon={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Kr^{\alpha}\big[(1+\alpha)\sin\big(\tau\theta+\omega\big)\sin\theta+\tau\cos\big(\tau\theta+\omega\big)\cos\theta\big]},\end{split}

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}\hat{\Delta}\upsilon&=Kr^{\alpha-1}\big((1+\alpha)^{2}-\tau^{2}\big){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sin\big(\tau\theta+\omega\big)}\\[5.0pt]
&=-\frac{K(3\alpha+5)(1-\alpha)}{4}r^{\alpha-1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sin\big(\tau\theta+\omega\big)},\end{split}

(4.63)

where
\hat{\Delta}:=\hat{\partial}^{2}_{11}+\hat{\partial}^{2}_{22}
.

Now let us consider the boundary value problem (
4.1
) near the corner
\mathcal{O}
.

By the estimates (
4.27
), (
4.47
) and the condition (
3.27
), for
\varepsilon_{\rm e}\in(\mathcal{C}_{\rm e,0}\tilde{\epsilon},\varepsilon_{\rm e,0})
, we can show that

\displaystyle\begin{split}|\hat{f}^{(\rm n)}|\leq\mathcal{C}\tilde{\epsilon}\varepsilon_{\rm e}r^{2\alpha-1}\leq\mathcal{C}\varepsilon^{2}_{\rm e}r^{2\alpha-1}.\end{split}

(4.64)

By (
4.46
)-(
4.47
) and the estimates (
4.27
) and (
4.19
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathscr{L}^{(\rm n)}\upsilon-\hat{f}^{(\rm n)}\\[5.0pt]
&\ \ =\hat{\Delta}\upsilon+(\mathscr{L}^{(\rm n)}-\hat{\Delta})\upsilon-\hat{f}^{(\rm n)}\\[5.0pt]
&\ \ =-\frac{K(3\alpha+5)(1-\alpha)}{4}r^{\alpha-1}\sin\big(\tau\theta+\omega\big)+\sum_{i,j=1,2}\Big(\hat{\mathcal{N}}^{(\rm n)}_{ij}-\delta_{ij}\Big)\hat{\partial}^{2}_{ij}\upsilon+\sum_{k,j=1,2}\hat{b}^{(\rm n)}_{kj}\hat{\partial}_{k}\upsilon\hat{\partial}_{j}\upsilon-\hat{f}^{(\rm n)}\\[5.0pt]
&\ \ \leq-\frac{\hat{K}(3\alpha+5)(1-\alpha)}{4}r^{-1-\alpha}_{0}r^{\alpha-1}\varepsilon_{\rm e}\sin\big(\tau\theta+\omega\big)\\
&\qquad\qquad+\mathcal{C}\hat{K}r^{-1-\alpha}_{0}r^{\alpha-1}\varepsilon^{2}_{\rm e}+\mathcal{C}\hat{K}^{2}r^{-2(1+\alpha)}_{0}r^{2\alpha}\varepsilon^{2}_{\rm e}+\mathcal{C}r^{2\alpha-1}\varepsilon^{2}_{\rm e}\\[5.0pt]
&\ \ =r^{-1-\alpha}_{0}r^{\alpha-1}\varepsilon_{\rm e}\bigg(-\frac{\hat{K}(3\alpha+5)(1-\alpha)}{4}\sin\big(\tau\theta+\omega\big)+\mathcal{C}\hat{K}\varepsilon_{\rm e}+\mathcal{C}\hat{K}^{2}r^{-1-\alpha}_{0}r^{\alpha+1}\varepsilon_{\rm e}+\mathcal{C}r^{\alpha+1}_{0}r^{\alpha}\varepsilon_{\rm e}\bigg)\\[5.0pt]
&\ \ =r^{-1-\alpha}_{0}r^{\alpha-1}\varepsilon_{\rm e}\bigg(-\frac{\hat{K}(3\alpha+5)(1-\alpha)}{4}\sin\big(\tau\theta+\omega\big)+\mathcal{C}\hat{K}\varepsilon_{\rm e}+\mathcal{C}\hat{K}^{2}\varepsilon_{\rm e}+\mathcal{C}r^{2\alpha+1}_{0}\varepsilon_{\rm e}\bigg),\end{split}

where
\mathcal{C}>0
depends only on the background state
\underline{U}^{(\rm e)}
.
Due to the choice of the constants
\alpha,\theta
and
\omega
, we know that

\displaystyle-\frac{\hat{K}(3\alpha+5)(1-\alpha)}{4}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sin\big(\tau\theta+\omega\big)}<0.

Thus, for
\varepsilon_{\rm e}<\varepsilon_{\rm e,0}
and
\hat{K}
sufficiently large, it holds that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathscr{L}^{(\rm n)}\upsilon-\hat{f}^{(\rm n)}\leq 0.\end{split}

(4.65)

Next, let us consider the boundary conditions. Obviously, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left.\hat{\psi}^{(\rm n)}\right.\big|_{\partial B^{+}_{r_{0}}\cap\hat{\Omega}^{(\rm e)}}\leq\left.\upsilon\right.\big|_{\partial B^{+}_{r_{0}}\cap\hat{\Omega}^{(\rm e)}},\end{split}

(4.66)

by letting
\hat{K}
sufficiently large.

On the boundary
\partial B^{+}_{r_{0}}\cap\hat{\Gamma}_{\rm cd}
,
due to the
C^{0,\alpha}
regularity of
\hat{\psi}^{(\rm n)}
, the Dirichlet condition
for
\hat{\psi}^{(\rm n)}
on
\hat{\Gamma}_{\rm cd}
can be extended to the origin,
thus one has

\displaystyle\begin{split}\hat{\psi}^{(\rm n)}-\upsilon&\leq\mathcal{C}r^{-1-\alpha}_{0}r^{1+\alpha}\Big(\mathcal{C}r^{1+\alpha}_{0}\|\omega_{\rm cd}\|^{(-\alpha,\{\mathcal{P}_{\rm e}\})}_{1,\alpha;\tilde{\Gamma}_{\rm cd}}-\hat{K}\varepsilon_{\rm e}\sin\omega\Big)\\[5.0pt]
&\leq\mathcal{C}r^{-1-\alpha}_{0}r^{1+\alpha}\Big(\mathcal{C}C_{w}r^{1+\alpha}_{0}\varrho-\hat{K}\varepsilon_{\rm e}\sin\omega\Big)\\[5.0pt]
&\leq\mathcal{C}r^{-1-\alpha}_{0}r^{1+\alpha}\varepsilon_{\rm e}\Big(\mathcal{C}r^{1+\alpha}_{0}-\hat{K}\sin\omega\Big)\\[5.0pt]
&\leq 0,\end{split}

(4.67)

by choosing
\varrho\in(0,\mathcal{C}^{-1}_{\rm e}\varepsilon_{\rm e})
and
\hat{K}
sufficiently large.

Finally, on
\partial B^{+}_{r_{0}}\cap{\hat{\Gamma}^{(\rm e)}_{\rm in}}
,

by the estimate (
4.27
) and the assumptions (
3.27
),
we have, for
\tilde{\epsilon}>0
sufficiently small and
\tilde{\epsilon}<\varepsilon_{\rm e}\in(\mathcal{C}_{\rm e}\tilde{\epsilon},\varepsilon_{\rm e,0})
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}|\hat{g}^{(\rm n)}|\leq\mathcal{C}\tilde{\epsilon}r^{\alpha}\leq\mathcal{C}\varepsilon_{\rm e}r^{\alpha}.\end{split}

(4.68)

Then, by (
4.68
), we can deduce that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathscr{M}^{(\rm n)}(\upsilon)-\hat{g}^{(\rm n)}\\[5.0pt]
=&\left.\hat{\beta}^{(\rm n)}_{0,1}Kr^{\alpha}\big[(1+\alpha)\sin\big(\tau\theta+\omega\big)\cos\theta-\tau\cos\big(\tau\theta+\omega\big)\sin\theta\big]\right.\Big|_{\theta=\frac{\pi}{2}}\\[5.0pt]
&+\left.\hat{\beta}^{(\rm n)}_{0,2}Kr^{\alpha}\big[(1+\alpha)\sin\big(\tau\theta+\omega\big)\sin\theta+\tau\cos\big(\tau\theta+\omega\big)\cos\theta\big]\right.\Big|_{\theta=\frac{\pi}{2}}-\hat{g}^{(\rm n)}\\[5.0pt]
\leq&\hat{K}\Big(-\frac{3+\alpha}{2}\hat{\beta}^{(\rm n)}_{0,1}\cos(\frac{\tau}{2}\pi+\omega)+(1+\alpha)\hat{\beta}^{(\rm n)}_{0,2}\sin(\frac{\tau}{2}\pi+\omega)\Big)r^{-1-\alpha}_{0}r^{\alpha}\varepsilon_{\rm e}+\mathcal{C}\varepsilon_{\rm e}r^{\alpha}\\[5.0pt]
=&\bigg(-\frac{\hat{K}(3+\alpha)}{2}\hat{\beta}^{(\rm n)}_{0,1}\big|_{\varphi^{(\rm n)}=\underline{\varphi},\tilde{B}^{(\rm e)}_{0}=\underline{B}^{(\rm e)},\tilde{S}^{(\rm e)}_{0}=\underline{S}^{(\rm e)}}\cos(\frac{\tau}{2}\pi+\omega)+\mathcal{C}\varepsilon_{\rm e}+\mathcal{C}r^{1+\alpha}_{0}\bigg)r^{-1-\alpha}_{0}r^{\alpha}\varepsilon_{\rm e}.\end{split}

(4.69)

By choosing
r_{0}=\varepsilon_{\rm e}<\varepsilon_{\rm e,0}
, and noting that
r\leq r_{0}
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathscr{M}^{(\rm n)}(\upsilon)-\hat{g}^{(\rm n)}\leq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-\frac{\hat{K}(3+\alpha)}{2}\hat{\beta}^{(\rm n)}_{0,1}\big|_{\varphi^{(\rm n)}=\underline{\varphi},\tilde{B}^{(\rm e)}_{0}=\underline{B}^{(\rm e)},\tilde{S}^{(\rm e)}_{0}=\underline{S}^{(\rm e)}}\cos(\frac{\tau}{2}\pi+\omega)+\mathcal{C}\varepsilon_{\rm e}.}\end{split}

(4.70)

We can take
0<\alpha_{0}<1
and
\hat{K}>0
sufficiently large depending only on
\tilde{\underline{U}}
and
L
so that

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-\hat{K}(1+\alpha)\hat{\beta}^{(\rm n)}_{0,1}\big|_{\varphi^{(\rm n)}=\underline{\varphi},\tilde{B}^{(\rm e)}_{0}=\underline{B}^{(\rm e)},\tilde{S}^{(\rm e)}_{0}=\underline{S}^{(\rm e)}}\cos\left(\frac{\tau}{2}\pi+\omega\right)+\mathcal{C}\varepsilon_{\rm e}\leq 0,}\end{split}

holds for any
\alpha\in(0,\alpha_{0})
, where we have used (
4.53
) and the fact:

\cos\left(\frac{\tau}{2}\pi+\omega\right)=\cos\left(\frac{7+\alpha}{8}\pi\right)<0
. It implies that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathscr{M}^{(\rm n)}(\upsilon)-\hat{g}^{(\rm n)}\leq 0.\end{split}

Then, by the maximal principle, we have
\psi^{(\rm n)}\leq\upsilon
.
Similarly, we can also obtain
\psi^{(\rm n)}\geq-\upsilon
. Thus, we have
|\psi^{(\rm n)}|\leq Kr^{1+\alpha}
.
Using the standard scaling technique, we can get
C^{1,\alpha}
-estimate for
\psi^{(n)}
, and then
C^{2,\alpha}
-estimate for
\varphi^{(\rm n)}
up to the corner point
\mathcal{O}
.
∎

4.2.
Estimates of solutions to the problem
(\widetilde{\mathbf{FP}})_{\rm n}
in the supersonic region

\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h)}

In this subsection, we will consider the solution
\delta z^{(\rm n)}
for the initial-boundary value problem

(\widetilde{\mathbf{FP}})_{\rm n}
in the supersonic region
\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h)}
:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{llll}\partial_{\xi}\delta z^{(\rm n)}+\textrm{diag}(\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{+},\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{-})\partial_{\eta}\delta z^{(\rm n)}=0,&\ \ \ \mbox{in}\ \ \ \tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h)},\\[5.0pt]
\delta z^{(\rm n)}=\delta z_{0}(\eta),&\ \ \ \mbox{on}\ \ \ \tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm h)}_{\rm in},\\[5.0pt]
\delta z^{(\rm n)}_{-}-\delta z^{(\rm n)}_{+}=2\beta^{(\rm n-1)}_{\rm cd,1}\partial_{\xi}\delta\varphi^{(\rm n)}+2\beta^{(\rm n-1)}_{\rm cd,2}\partial_{\eta}\delta\varphi^{(\rm n)}+2c_{\rm cd}(\xi),&\ \ \ \mbox{on}\ \ \ \tilde{\Gamma}_{\rm cd},\\[5.0pt]
\delta z^{(\rm n)}_{-}+\delta z^{(\rm n)}_{+}=2\arctan g^{\prime}_{-}(\xi),&\ \ \ \mbox{on}\ \ \ \tilde{\Gamma}_{-},\end{array}\right.

where the functions
\beta^{(\rm n-1)}_{\rm cd,1}
,
\beta^{(\rm n-1)}_{\rm cd,2}
and
c_{\rm cd}(\xi)

are defined in
(
3.103
)-(
3.104
)
. We have the following result.

Proposition 4.2
.

For any given
\alpha\in(0,1)
,
there exist constants
\mathcal{C}^{*}_{\rm h,0}>0
and
\epsilon^{*}_{\rm h,0}>0
depending only on
\underline{\tilde{U}}
,
L
and
\alpha
, such that for

\tilde{\epsilon}<\epsilon_{I}\in(\mathcal{C}^{*}_{\rm h,0}\tilde{\epsilon},\epsilon^{*}_{\rm h,0})
with
\tilde{\epsilon}>0
is sufficiently small,
if
(\delta z^{(\rm n-1)},\delta\varphi^{(\rm n-1)})\in\mathscr{K}_{2\epsilon_{I}}
,
then
the solution
z^{(\rm n)}
to the problem (
4.2
) satisfies

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\|\delta z^{(\rm n)}_{-}\|_{1,\alpha;\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h)}}+\|\delta z^{(\rm n)}_{+}\|_{1,\alpha;\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h)}}\\[5.0pt]
\leq&C^{*}_{41}\bigg(\big\|\delta z^{(\rm n)}_{0}\big\|_{1,\alpha;\tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm h)}_{\rm in}}+\sum_{k=\rm e,h}\|\delta\tilde{B}^{(\rm k)}_{0}\|_{1,\alpha;\tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm k)}_{\rm in}}\\[5.0pt]
&\qquad\qquad+\sum_{k=\rm e,h}\|\delta\tilde{S}^{(\rm k)}_{0}\|_{1,\alpha;\tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm k)}_{\rm in}}+\big\|g_{-}+1\big\|_{2,\alpha;\tilde{\Gamma}_{-}}+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\big\|\delta\varphi^{(\rm n)}\big\|^{(-1-\alpha,\{\mathcal{P}_{\rm e}\})}_{2,\alpha;\overline{\tilde{\Gamma}}_{\rm cd}}}\bigg),\end{split}

(4.76)

where
C^{*}_{41}>0
depends only on
\underline{\tilde{U}}
,
L
and
\alpha
.

\xi=L
\xi=0
\eta=0
\eta=-m^{(\rm h)}
\xi^{*}_{\rm cd,0}
\xi^{*}_{\rm cd,1}
\xi^{*}_{-m^{(\rm h)},0}
\xi^{*}_{-m^{(\rm h)},1}
l^{0}_{-}
l^{0}_{+}
l^{1}_{+}
l^{1}_{-}
\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h)}
\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h)}_{I}
\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h)}_{II}
\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h)}_{III}
\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h)}_{IV}
\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h)}_{V}
\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h)}_{VI}
\tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm h)}_{\rm in}
\tilde{\Gamma}_{-}
\tilde{\Gamma}_{\rm cd}

Fig. 4.6.
Initial-boundary value problem for
(\widetilde{\mathbf{FP}})_{\rm n}
in
\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h)}

The proof of the Proposition
4.2
will be divided into several steps to deal with the different sub-domains
which are constructed as follows (see Fig.
4.6
). Denoted by
\tilde{\Omega}_{I}^{(\rm h)}
the triangle which is bounded by the inlet
\tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm h)}_{\rm in}
, the characteristics of (
4.2
) with the speed
\lambda_{+}
issuing from the point
(0,-m^{(\rm h)})
(which we denote by
l^{0}_{+}
) and another characteristics of (
4.2
) which corresponding to
\lambda_{-}
issuing from the point
(0,0)
(which we denote by
l_{-}^{0}
).
Let
\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h)}_{II}
be the triangle bounded by
l^{0}_{\pm}
and the contact discontinuity
\tilde{\Gamma}_{\rm cd}
. Let
\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h)}_{III}
be the triangle bounded by the lower nozzle walls
\tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm h)}_{-}
,
l_{+}^{0}
and
l_{-}^{0}
.
Let
\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h)}_{IV}
be the diamond bounded by
l^{0}_{\pm}
, the characteristics
l^{1}_{+}
determined by
\lambda_{+}
issuing from the point where
l_{-}^{0}
and the lower nozzle walls
\tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm h)}_{-}

intersect,
as well as the characteristics
l^{1}_{-}
with the speed
\lambda_{-}
issuing from the intersection point of
l_{+}^{0}
and the contact discontinuity
\tilde{\Gamma}_{\rm cd}
. Let
\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h)}_{V}
be the triangle bounded by
l^{1}_{+}
,
l^{1}_{-}
and the contact discontinuity
\tilde{\Gamma}_{\rm cd}
.
Let
\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h)}_{VI}
be the triangle bounded by
l^{1}_{-}
,
l^{1}_{+}
and the lower nozzle wall
\tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm h)}_{-}
.
Finally, we remark that different from the argument made in
[
36
]
, we need to consider the Hölder estimates,
i.e.
,
C^{1,\alpha}
-norm on the charateristic curves and the solutions instead of the
C^{1}
-norm used in
[
36
]
.

4.2.1.
Estimates on the characteristic curves

First, let us introduce several lemmas on the Hölder regularity of the characteristic curves.
For any
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\eta_{\pm,0}}\in[-m^{(\rm h)},0]
, let
\eta=\phi^{(\rm n)}_{\pm}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\xi,\eta_{\pm,0}})
be the characteristic curves corresponding to
\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{\pm}
issuing from the point
(0,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\eta_{\pm,0}})
:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left\{\begin{array}[]{llll}\frac{d\phi^{(\rm n)}_{\pm}}{d\xi}=\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{\pm}(\xi,\phi^{(\rm n)}_{\pm}(\tau,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\eta_{\pm,0}})),\\[5.0pt]
\phi^{(\rm n)}_{\pm}(0,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\eta_{\pm,0}})={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\eta_{\pm,0}}.\end{array}\right.\end{split}

(4.77)

Along the characteristics, we have

\displaystyle\eta={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\eta_{\pm,0}}+\int^{\xi}_{0}\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{\pm}(\tau,\phi^{(\rm n)}_{\pm}(\tau,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\eta_{\pm,0}}))d\tau.

(4.78)

From (
4.78
), we can regard
\eta_{+,0}
(or
\eta_{-,0}
) as a function of
(\xi,\eta)
,

i.e.
,
\eta_{+,0}=\eta_{+,0}(\xi,\eta)
(or
\eta_{-,0}=\eta_{-,0}(\xi,\eta)
).

For any two points
P=(\xi_{P},\eta_{P}),\ Q=(\xi_{Q},\eta_{Q})\in\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h)}_{I}\cup\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h)}_{II}

(or
P,\,Q\in\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h)}_{I}\cup\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h)}_{III}
), there exist unique
points
P_{+}(0)=(0,\eta_{P_{+}(0)}),Q_{+}(0)=(0,\eta_{Q_{+}(0)})\in\tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm h)}_{\rm in}

(or
P_{-}(0)=(0,\eta_{P_{-}(0)}),Q_{-}(0)=(0,\eta_{Q_{-}(0)})\in\tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm h)}_{\rm in}
)
such that the characteristics corresponding to
\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{+}
(or
\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{-}
)
issuing from
P_{+}(0)
and
Q_{+}(0)
(or
P_{-}(0)
and
Q_{-}(0)
) pass through
P
and
Q
.

Define
P_{+}(\xi)=(\xi,\phi_{+}(\xi,\eta_{P_{+}(0)}))
and
Q_{+}(\xi)=(\xi,\phi_{+}(\xi,\eta_{Q_{+}(0)}))

(or
P_{-}(\xi)=(\xi,\phi_{-}(\xi,\eta_{P_{-}(0)}))
and
Q_{-}(\xi)=(\xi,\phi_{-}(\xi,\eta_{Q_{-}(0)}))
). Then

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\eta_{P_{+}(\xi)}=\phi_{+}(\xi,\eta_{P_{+}(0)})=\eta_{P_{+}(0)}+\int^{\xi}_{0}\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{+}(P_{+}(\tau))d\tau,\\[5.0pt]
&\eta_{Q_{+}(\xi)}=\phi_{+}(\xi,\eta_{Q_{+}(0)})=\eta_{Q_{+}(0)}+\int^{\xi}_{0}\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{+}(Q_{+}(\tau))d\tau,\end{split}

(4.79)

for
P_{+},Q_{+}\in\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h)}_{I}\cup\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h)}_{II}
, (or

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\eta_{P_{-}(\xi)}=\phi_{-}(\xi,\eta_{P_{-}(0)})=\eta_{P_{-}(0)}+\int^{\xi}_{0}\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{-}(P_{-}(\tau))d\tau,\\[5.0pt]
&\eta_{Q_{-}(\xi)}=\phi_{-}(\xi,\eta_{Q_{-}(0)})=\eta_{Q_{-}(0)}+\int^{\xi}_{0}\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{-}(Q_{-}(\tau))d\tau,\end{split}

(4.80)

for
P_{-},Q_{-}\in\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h)}_{I}\cup\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h)}_{III}
).

From the above arguments, we also have
\eta_{P_{\pm}(0)}=\eta_{\pm,0}(P)
and
\eta_{Q_{\pm}(0)}=\eta_{\pm,0}(Q)
.
Finally, we define the distance in the Euclidean norm as

\displaystyle d(P_{\pm}(\xi),Q_{\pm}(\xi))=\big|P_{\pm}(\xi)-Q_{\pm}(\xi)\big|,\ d^{\alpha}(P_{\pm}(\xi),Q_{\pm}(\xi))=\big|P_{\pm}(\xi)-Q_{\pm}(\xi)\big|^{\alpha},\ \forall\alpha\in(0,1).

(4.81)

Then, we have

Lemma 4.1
.

For any
\alpha\in(0,1)
and
\delta z^{(\rm n-1)}\in\mathscr{K}_{2\epsilon_{I}}
,
there exist constants
\mathcal{C}_{\rm h,1}>0
and
\epsilon_{\rm h,1}>0

C_{4i},(i=1,2,3)
depending only on
\tilde{\underline{U}}
,
L
and
\alpha
, such that
for
\tilde{\epsilon}<\epsilon_{I}\in(\mathcal{C}_{\rm h,1}\tilde{\epsilon},\epsilon_{\rm h,1})
with
\tilde{\epsilon}>0
sufficiently small, one has

\displaystyle\begin{split}(\rm i)\ \ &d^{\alpha}\big({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{\pm}}(\xi),{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q_{\pm}}(\xi)\big)\leq C_{41}d^{\alpha}\big(P,Q\big),\quad&\xi\in[0,\xi_{PQ}],\\[5.0pt]
(\rm ii)\ \ &d^{\alpha}\big({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{\pm}}(\xi),{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q_{\pm}}(\xi)\big)\leq C_{42}d^{\alpha}\big({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{\pm}}(\xi_{PQ}),{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q_{\pm}}(\xi_{PQ})\big),\quad&\xi\in[0,\xi_{PQ}],\\[5.0pt]
(\rm iii)\ \ &{C^{-1}_{43}|\xi_{PQ}-\xi_{Q}|^{\alpha}\leq d^{\alpha}\big({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q_{\pm}(\xi_{PQ})},Q\big)\leq C_{43}|\xi_{PQ}-\xi_{Q}|^{\alpha},}\end{split}

(4.82)

for any
P,Q\in\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h)}_{I}\cup\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h)}_{II}
(or
P,Q\in\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h)}_{I}\cup\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h)}_{III}
),
where
\xi_{PQ}=\min\{\xi_{P},\ \xi_{Q}\}
,
P_{\pm}(\xi)=\big(\xi,\phi^{(\rm n)}_{\pm}(\xi,\eta_{P_{\pm}(0)})\big),Q_{\pm}(\xi)=\big(\xi,\phi^{(\rm n)}_{\pm}(\xi,\eta_{Q_{\pm}(0)})\big)
,
P_{\pm}(\xi_{PQ})=\big(\xi_{PQ},\phi^{(\rm n)}_{\pm}(\xi_{PQ},\eta_{P_{\pm}(0)})\big)
, and
\ Q_{\pm}(\xi_{PQ})=\big(\xi_{PQ},\phi^{(\rm n)}_{\pm}(\xi_{PQ},\eta_{Q_{\pm}(0)})\big)
.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we only consider the case that
P,Q\in\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h)}_{I}\cup\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h)}_{II}
and
\xi_{PQ}=\xi_{P}
, since the other cases can be treated similarly.
By (
4.77
), we know that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{\partial}{\partial\xi}\Big(\frac{\partial\phi^{(\rm n)}_{+}}{\partial\eta_{+,0}}\Big)=\partial_{\eta}\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{+}\frac{\partial\phi^{(\rm n)}_{+}}{\partial\eta_{+,0}},\ \ \ \frac{\partial\phi^{(\rm n)}_{+}}{\partial\eta_{+,0}}(0,\eta_{+,0})=1,\end{split}

which leads to

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{\partial\phi^{(\rm n)}_{+}}{\partial\eta_{+,0}}=e^{\int^{\xi}_{0}\partial_{\eta}\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{+}(\tau,\phi^{(\rm n)}_{+}(\tau,\eta_{+,0}))d\tau}.\end{split}

Noticing that

\displaystyle\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{+}\big|_{\delta z^{(\rm n-1)}_{+}=\delta\tilde{B}^{(\rm h)}_{0}=\delta\tilde{S}^{(\rm h)}_{0}=0}=-\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{-}\big|_{\delta z^{(\rm n-1)}_{-}=\delta\tilde{B}^{(\rm h)}_{0}=\delta\tilde{S}^{(\rm h)}_{0}=0}=\underline{\lambda}_{+}>0,

(4.83)

one has

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{\pm}-\underline{\lambda}_{\pm}\|_{1,\alpha;\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h)}}&\leq\mathcal{C}\Big(\|\delta z^{(\rm n-1)}\|_{1,\alpha;\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h)}}+\|\delta\tilde{B}^{(\rm h)}_{0}\|_{1,\alpha;\tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm h)}_{\rm in}}+\|\delta\tilde{S}^{(\rm h)}_{0}\|_{1,\alpha;\tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm h)}_{\rm in}}\Big)\\[5.0pt]
&\leq\mathcal{C}(2\epsilon_{I}+\tilde{\epsilon}),\end{split}

(4.84)

and hence there exist constants
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{\rm h,1}>0
and
\epsilon^{\prime}_{\rm h,1}>0
depending only on
\tilde{\underline{U}}
and
L
, such that
for
\tilde{\epsilon}>0
sufficiently small, if
\tilde{\epsilon}<\epsilon_{I}\in(\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{\rm h,1}\tilde{\epsilon},\epsilon^{\prime}_{\rm h,1})
,
the following holds,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{1}{2}\underline{\lambda}_{+}<\|\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{\pm}\|_{1,\alpha;\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h)}}<\frac{3}{2}\underline{\lambda}_{+},\qquad\frac{1}{2}\underline{\lambda}_{+}\leq\pm\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{\pm}<\frac{3}{2}\underline{\lambda}_{+}.\end{split}

(4.85)

Then, by (
4.85
), it follows that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\big|\phi^{(\rm n)}_{+}(\xi,\eta_{P_{+}(0)})-\phi^{(\rm n)}_{+}(\xi,\eta_{Q_{+}(0)})|\leq\mathcal{C}|\eta_{P_{+}(0)}-\eta_{Q_{+}(0)}|,\end{split}

(4.86)

for
0\leq\xi\leq\xi_{PQ}
.
On the other hand, from (
4.79
) and (
4.84
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\big|\eta_{P_{+}(0)}-\eta_{Q_{+}(0)}\big|&\leq\big|\eta_{P}-\eta_{Q}\big|+\bigg|\int^{\xi_{Q}}_{\xi_{P}}\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{+}\big(Q_{+}(\tau)\big)d\tau\bigg|\\[5.0pt]
&\ \ \ \ +\Bigg|\int^{\xi_{P}}_{0}\Big(\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{+}\big(Q_{+}(\tau)\big)-\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{+}\big(P_{+}(\tau)\big)\Big)d\tau\Bigg|\\[5.0pt]
&\leq\mathcal{C}L\bigg(\big\|Dz^{(\rm n-1)}\big\|_{0,0;\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h)}}+\big\|\tilde{B}^{(\rm h)^{\prime}}_{0}\big\|_{0,0;\tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm h)}_{\rm in}}+\big\|\tilde{S}^{(\rm h)^{\prime}}_{0}\big\|_{0,0;\tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm h)}_{\rm in}}\bigg)\big|\eta_{P_{+}(0)}-\eta_{Q_{+}(0)}\big|\\[5.0pt]
&\ \ \ \ +\big|\eta_{P}-\eta_{Q}\big|+\mathcal{C}\big|\xi_{P}-\xi_{Q}\big|\\[5.0pt]
&\leq\mathcal{C}\Big(\big|\xi_{P}-\xi_{Q}\big|+\big|\eta_{P}-\eta_{Q}\big|\Big)+2\mathcal{C}L\big(2\epsilon_{I}+\tilde{\epsilon}\big)\big|\eta_{P_{+}(0)}-\eta_{Q_{+}(0)}\big|.\end{split}

(4.87)

By choosing constants
\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}_{\rm h,1}>0
and
\epsilon^{\prime\prime}_{\rm h,1}>0
depending only on
\tilde{\underline{U}}
and
L
, so that
for
\tilde{\epsilon}>0
sufficiently small and
\tilde{\epsilon}<\epsilon_{I}\in(\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}_{\rm h,1}\tilde{\epsilon},\epsilon^{\prime\prime}_{\rm h,1})
, we have
2\mathcal{C}L\big(2\epsilon_{I}+\tilde{\epsilon}\big)<\frac{1}{2}
.
Then, it follows from (
4.87
) that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\big|\eta_{P_{+}(0)}-\eta_{Q_{+}(0)}\big|\leq 2\mathcal{C}\Big(\big|\xi_{P}-\xi_{Q}\big|+\big|\eta_{P}-\eta_{Q}\big|\Big).\end{split}

(4.88)

Combining the estimates (
4.86
) and (
4.88
), we get the first estimate (i)
in (
4.82
).

Next, taking
\xi_{P}=\xi_{Q}
in (
4.87
) and then applying estimates (
4.86
) and (
4.88
) again,
we have the second estimate (ii)
in (
4.82
).

Finally, by (
4.79
) and the estimate (
4.85
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}d\big(Q_{+}(\xi_{P}),Q\big)=\big|\xi_{P}-\xi_{Q}\big|+\bigg|\int^{\xi_{Q}}_{\xi_{P}}\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{+}\big(Q_{+}(\tau)\big)d\tau\bigg|\leq\mathcal{C}\big|\xi_{P}-\xi_{Q}\big|,\end{split}

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}d\big(Q_{+}(\xi_{P}),Q\big)=\big|\xi_{P}-\xi_{Q}\big|+\bigg|\int^{\xi_{Q}}_{\xi_{P}}\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{+}\big(Q_{+}(\tau)\big)d\tau\bigg|\geq\mathcal{C}^{-1}\big|\xi_{P}-\xi_{Q}\big|.\end{split}

Then the estimate (iii) in (
4.82
) follows.
Finally, we take
\mathcal{C}_{\rm h,1}=\max\{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{\rm h,1},\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}_{\rm h,1}\}
and
\epsilon_{\rm h,1}=\min\{\epsilon^{\prime}_{\rm h,1},\epsilon^{\prime\prime}_{\rm h,1}\}
,
then, if
\tilde{\epsilon}>0
is sufficiently small and
\tilde{\epsilon}<\epsilon_{I}\in(\mathcal{C}_{\rm h,1}\tilde{\epsilon},\epsilon_{\rm h,1})
, the results of the lemma hold.
∎

Now, we turn to the estimates for
\eta_{\pm,0}
given by (
4.78
). For any point
(\xi,\eta)\in\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h)}_{I}\cap\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h)}_{II}
(or
(\xi,\eta)\in\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h)}_{I}\cap\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h)}_{III}
), there exists a unique
\eta_{+,0}
,
such that the characteristic curve corresponding to
\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{+}
(or
\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{-}
) issuing from
(0,\eta_{+,0})
(or
(0,\eta_{-,0})
) passes through
(\xi,\eta)
.
Hence, by (
4.85
) and the implicit function theorem, from (
4.78
) we can regard
\eta_{+,0}
(or
\eta_{+,0}
) as a function of
(\xi,\eta)
,

i.e.
,
\eta_{+,0}=\eta_{+,0}(\xi,\eta)
(or
\eta_{-,0}=\eta_{-,0}(\xi,\eta)
).

Lemma 4.2
.

For any
\alpha\in(0,1)
and
\delta z^{(\rm n-1)}\in\mathscr{K}_{2\epsilon_{I}}
,

there exist constants
\epsilon_{\rm h,2}>0
,
\mathcal{C}_{\rm h,2}>0
and

C_{44}>0
depending only on
\tilde{\underline{U}}
,
L
and
\alpha
, such that for
\tilde{\epsilon}>0
sufficiently small and
\tilde{\epsilon}<\epsilon_{I}\in(\mathcal{C}_{\rm h,2}\tilde{\epsilon},\epsilon_{\rm h,2})
,

it holds that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|D\eta_{+,0}\|_{0,\alpha;\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h)}_{I}\cup\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h)}_{II}}+\|D\eta_{-,0}\|_{0,\alpha;\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h)}_{I}\cup\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h)}_{III}}\leq C_{44}.\end{split}

(4.89)

Proof.

We only prove the estimate for
D\eta_{+,0}
in
\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h)}_{I}\cup\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h)}_{II}
, since the estimate for
D\eta_{-,0}
in
\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h)}_{I}\cup\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h)}_{III}
can be treated in the same way.
First,
\|D\eta_{+,0}\|_{0,0;\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h)}_{I}\cup\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h)}_{II}}
can be estimated easily by choosing constants
\mathcal{C}_{\rm h,2}>0
and
\epsilon_{\rm h,2}>0
depending only on
\tilde{\underline{U}}
and
L
such that
\tilde{\epsilon}<\epsilon_{I}\in(\mathcal{C}_{\rm h,2}\tilde{\epsilon},\epsilon_{\rm h,2})
for
\tilde{\epsilon}>0
sufficiently small as done in
[
36
, Lemma 4.1]
.
So it remains to estimate
[D\eta_{+,0}]_{0,\alpha;\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h}_{I}\cup\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h)}_{II})}
.
Taking derivatives on (
4.78
) with respect to
\xi
and
\eta
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\frac{\partial\eta_{+,0}}{\partial\xi}=-\frac{\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{+}(\xi,\eta)}{1+\int^{\xi}_{0}\partial_{\eta}\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{+}e^{\int^{\tau}_{0}\partial_{\eta}\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{+}ds}d\tau},\\[5.0pt]
&\frac{\partial\eta_{+,0}}{\partial\eta}=\frac{1}{1+\int^{\xi}_{0}\partial_{\eta}\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{+}e^{\int^{\tau}_{0}\partial_{\eta}\lambda^{(\rm-1)}_{+}ds}d\tau}.\end{split}

(4.90)

Taking two points
P_{1}=(\xi_{1},\eta_{1})
and
P_{2}=(\xi_{2},\eta_{2})
, which satisfy (
4.79
)
with initial conditions
\eta_{P_{1,+}(0)}=\eta_{+,0}(P_{1})
and
\eta_{P_{2,+}(0)}=\eta_{+,0}(P_{2})
,
respectively.
Then we need to estimate the term
d^{-\alpha}(P_{1},P_{2})\big|D\eta_{+,0}(P_{1})-D\eta_{+,0}(P_{2})\big|
.
Notice that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&d^{-\alpha}(P_{1},P_{2})\big|D\eta_{+,0}(P_{1})-D\eta_{+,0}(P_{2})\big|\\[5.0pt]
&\ \ \ \leq d^{-\alpha}(P_{1},P_{2})\big|\partial_{\xi}\eta_{+,0}(P_{1})-\partial_{\xi}\eta_{+,0}(P_{2})\big|+d^{-\alpha}(P_{1},P_{2})\big|\partial_{\eta}\eta_{+,0}(P_{1})-\partial_{\eta}\eta_{+,0}(P_{2})\big|\\[5.0pt]
&\ \ \ =:I_{1}+I_{2}.\end{split}

For
I_{1}
, by (
4.90
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}I_{1}&=d^{-\alpha}(P_{1},P_{2})\Bigg|\frac{\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{+}(P_{1})}{1+\int^{\xi_{1}}_{0}\partial_{\eta}\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{+}e^{\int^{\tau}_{0}\partial_{\eta}\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{+}ds}d\tau}-\frac{\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{+}(P_{2})}{1+\int^{\xi_{2}}_{0}\partial_{\eta}\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{+}e^{\int^{\tau}_{0}\partial_{\eta}\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{+}ds}d\tau}\Bigg|\\[5.0pt]
&\leq I_{11}+I_{12},\end{split}

where

\displaystyle\begin{split}I_{11}=\Bigg|\frac{d^{-\alpha}(P_{1},P_{2})\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{+}(P_{1})\Big(\int^{\xi_{2}}_{0}\partial_{\eta}\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{+}e^{\int^{\tau}_{0}\partial_{\eta}\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{+}ds}d\tau-\int^{\xi_{1}}_{0}\partial_{\eta}\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{+}e^{\int^{\tau}_{0}\partial_{\eta}\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{+}ds}d\tau\Big)}{\big(1+\int^{\xi_{1}}_{0}\partial_{\eta}\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{+}e^{\int^{\tau}_{0}\partial_{\eta}\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{+}ds}d\tau\big)\big(1+\int^{\xi_{2}}_{0}\partial_{\eta}\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{+}e^{\int^{\tau}_{0}\partial_{\eta}\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{+}ds}d\tau\big)}\Bigg|,\end{split}

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}I_{12}&=d^{-\alpha}(P_{1},P_{2})\Bigg|\Big(1+\int^{\xi_{1}}_{0}\partial_{\eta}\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{+}e^{\int^{\tau}_{0}\partial_{\eta}\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{+}ds}d\tau\Big)^{-1}\Bigg|\big|\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{+}(P_{1})-\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{+}(P_{2})\big|.\end{split}

By Lemma
4.1
and by choosing constants still denoted by
\mathcal{C}_{\rm h,2}>0
and
\epsilon_{\rm h,2}>0
depending only on
\tilde{\underline{U}}
,
L
and
\alpha
such that for

\tilde{\epsilon}<\epsilon_{I}\in(\mathcal{C}_{\rm h,2}\tilde{\epsilon},\epsilon_{\rm h,2})
with
\tilde{\epsilon}>0
is sufficiently small, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}I_{11}&=\mathcal{C}d^{-\alpha}(P_{1},P_{2})\bigg|\int^{\xi_{2}}_{\xi_{1}}\big(\partial_{\eta}\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{+}e^{\int^{\tau}_{0}\partial_{\eta}\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{+}ds}\big)(P_{2,+}(\tau))d\tau\bigg|\\[5.0pt]
&\ \ \ +\mathcal{C}d^{-\alpha}(P_{1},P_{2})\int^{\xi_{1}}_{0}\Big|\big(\partial_{\eta}\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{+}e^{\int^{\tau}_{0}\partial_{\eta}\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{+}ds}\big)(P_{1,+}(\tau))-\big(\partial_{\eta}\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{+}e^{\int^{\tau}_{0}\partial_{\eta}\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{+}ds}\big)(P_{2,+}(\tau))\Big|d\tau\\[5.0pt]
&\leq\mathcal{C},\end{split}

where
\mathcal{C}>0
depends only on
\tilde{\underline{U}}
,
L
and
\alpha
.
Similarly, we can get the estimates for
I_{12}
and
I_{2}
to obtain
I_{12}+I_{2}\leq\mathcal{C}
for some constant
\mathcal{C}>0
depending only on
\tilde{\underline{U}}
,
L
and
\alpha
.
Then, with the estimates for
I_{11}
,
I_{12}
and
I_{2}
, we have the desired estimate on
[D\eta_{+,0}]_{0,\alpha;\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h)}_{I}\cup\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h)}_{II}}
.
This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Next, let us consider the characteristic curves issuing from the lower nozzle wall. Let
\xi^{*}_{\rm cd,0}
be the
\xi
-coordinate of the intersection point of characteristic

\eta=\phi^{(\rm n)}_{+}(\xi,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-m^{(\rm h)}})

and the transonic contact discontinuity
\eta=0
, and let
\xi^{*}_{-m^{(\rm h)},0}
be the
\xi
-coordinate of the intersection point of the characteristics
\eta=\phi^{(\rm n)}_{-}(\xi,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}0})

and the nozzle wall
\eta=-m^{(\rm h)}
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int^{\xi^{*}_{\rm cd,0}}_{0}\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{+}(\tau,\phi^{(\rm n)}_{+}(\tau,-m^{(\rm h)}))d\tau=m^{(\rm h)},\\[5.0pt]
&\int^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\xi^{*}_{-m^{(\rm h)},0}}}_{0}\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{-}(\tau,\phi^{(\rm n)}_{-}(\tau,0))d\tau=-m^{(\rm h)}.\end{split}

(4.91)

Let
\xi=\chi^{(\rm n)}_{+}(\eta,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\xi_{-m^{(\rm h)},0}})
be the characteristic curves corresponding to
\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{+}
issuing from the point
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\xi_{-m^{(\rm h)},0}},-m^{(\rm h)})
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left\{\begin{array}[]{llll}\frac{d\chi^{(\rm n)}_{+}}{d\eta}=\frac{1}{\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{+}(\chi^{(\rm n)}_{+}(\eta,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\xi_{-m^{(\rm h)},0}}),\eta)},\\[5.0pt]
\chi^{(\rm n)}_{+}(-m^{(\rm h)},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\xi_{-m^{(\rm h)},0}})={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\xi_{-m^{(\rm h)},0}},\end{array}\right.\end{split}

(4.92)

for
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\xi_{-m^{(\rm h)},0}}\in[0,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\xi^{*}_{-m^{(\rm h)},0}}]
, where
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\xi^{*}_{-m^{(\rm h)},0}}
is given by (
4.91
).
Integrating (
4.92
) from
-m^{(\rm h)}
to
\eta
, one has

\displaystyle\begin{split}\xi={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\xi_{-m^{(\rm h)},0}}+\int^{\eta}_{-m^{(\rm h)}}\frac{d\tau}{\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{+}(\chi^{(\rm n)}_{+}(\tau,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\xi_{-m^{(\rm h)},0}}),\tau)}.\end{split}

(4.93)

Given any two points
\hat{P}=(\xi_{\hat{P}},\eta_{\hat{P}}),\ \hat{Q}=(\xi_{\hat{Q}},\eta_{\hat{Q}})\in\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h)}_{III}\cup\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h)}_{IV}\cup\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h)}_{V}
,
there exist unique points
\hat{P}_{+}(-m^{(\rm h)})=(\xi_{\hat{P}_{+}(-m^{(\rm h)})},-m^{(\rm h)})
and
\hat{Q}_{+}(-m^{(\rm h)})=(\xi_{\hat{Q}_{+}(-m^{(\rm h)})},-m^{(\rm h)})

such that the characteristics corresponding to
\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{+}
issuing from them pass through
\hat{P}
and
\hat{Q}
, respectively.
Define
\hat{P}_{+}(\eta)=(\chi^{(\rm n)}_{+}(\eta,\xi_{\hat{P}_{+}(-m^{(\rm h)})}),\eta)
and
\hat{Q}_{+}(\eta)=(\chi^{(\rm n)}_{+}(\eta,\xi_{\hat{Q}_{+}(-m^{(\rm h)})}),\eta)
. Then

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\xi_{\hat{P}_{+}(\eta)}=\chi^{(\rm n)}_{+}(\eta,\xi_{\hat{P}_{+}(-m^{(\rm h)})})=\xi_{\hat{P}_{+}(-m^{(\rm h)})}+\int^{\eta}_{-m^{(\rm h)}}\frac{d\tau}{\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{+}(\hat{P}_{+}(\tau))},\\[5.0pt]
&\xi_{\hat{Q}_{+}(\eta)}=\chi^{(\rm n)}_{+}(\eta,\xi_{\hat{Q}_{+}(-m^{(\rm h)})})=\xi_{\hat{Q}_{+}(-m^{(\rm h)})}+\int^{\eta}_{-m^{(\rm h)}}\frac{d\tau}{\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{+}(\hat{Q}_{+}(\tau))}.\end{split}

(4.94)

Now, we define the distance in the Euclidean norm for this case as

\displaystyle\begin{split}d\big(\hat{P}_{+}(\eta),\hat{Q}_{+}(\eta)\big)=\big|\hat{P}_{+}(\eta)-\hat{Q}_{+}(\eta)\big|,\quad\ d^{\alpha}\big(\hat{P}_{+}(\eta),\hat{Q}_{+}(\eta)\big)=\big|\hat{P}_{+}(\eta)-\hat{Q}_{+}(\eta)\big|^{\alpha}.\end{split}

(4.95)

We have the following lemma.

Lemma 4.3
.

For any
\alpha\in(0,1)
and
\delta z^{(\rm n-1)}\in\mathscr{K}_{2\epsilon_{I}}
,
there exist positive constants
\mathcal{C}_{\rm h,3}>0
,
\epsilon_{\rm h,3}>0
and
\hat{C}_{4j},(j=1,2,3)
depending only on
\tilde{\underline{U}}
,
L
and
\alpha
,

such that for
\tilde{\epsilon}>0
sufficiently small and
\tilde{\epsilon}<\epsilon_{I}\in(\mathcal{C}_{\rm h,3}\tilde{\epsilon},\epsilon_{\rm h,3})
,
we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}(\rm i)\ \ &d^{\alpha}\big(\hat{P}_{+}(\eta),\hat{Q}_{+}(\eta)\big)\leq\hat{C}_{41}d^{\alpha}\big(\hat{P},\hat{Q}\big),\quad&\eta\in[-m^{(\rm h)},\eta_{\hat{P}\hat{Q}}],\\[5.0pt]
(\rm ii)\ \ &d^{\alpha}\big(\hat{P}_{+}(\eta),\hat{Q}_{+}(\eta)\big)\leq\hat{C}_{42}d^{\alpha}\big(\hat{P}_{+}(\eta_{\hat{P}\hat{Q}}),\hat{Q}_{+}(\eta_{\hat{P}\hat{Q}})\big),\quad&\eta\in[-m^{(\rm h)},\eta_{\hat{P}\hat{Q}}],\\[5.0pt]
(\rm iii)\ \ &{\hat{C}^{-1}_{43}\big|\eta_{\hat{P}\hat{Q}}-\eta_{\hat{Q}}\big|^{\alpha}\leq d^{\alpha}\big(\hat{Q}_{+}(\eta_{\hat{P}\hat{Q}}),\hat{Q}\big)\leq\hat{C}_{43}\big|\eta_{\hat{P}\hat{Q}}-\eta_{\hat{Q}}\big|^{\alpha},}\end{split}

(4.96)

where
\eta_{\hat{P}\hat{Q}}=\min\{\eta_{\hat{P}},\ \eta_{\hat{Q}}\}
, and
\hat{P}_{+}(\eta_{\hat{P}\hat{Q}})=\big(\chi^{(\rm n)}_{+}(\eta_{\hat{P}\hat{Q}},\xi_{\hat{P}_{+}(-m^{(\rm h)})}),\eta_{\hat{P}\hat{Q}}\big)

and
\hat{Q}_{+}(\eta_{\hat{P}\hat{Q}})=\big(\chi^{(\rm n)}_{+}(\eta_{\hat{P}\hat{Q}},\xi_{\hat{Q}_{+}(-m^{(\rm h)})}),\eta_{\hat{P}\hat{Q}}\big)
.

Proof.

Without loss of the generality, we only consider the case that
\eta_{\hat{P}\hat{Q}}=\eta_{\hat{P}}
because the other case can be dealt similarly.
First, taking derivatives with respect to
\xi_{-m^{(\rm h)},0}
in (
4.93
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left\{\begin{array}[]{llll}\frac{\partial}{\partial\eta}\Big(\frac{\partial\chi^{(\rm n)}_{+}}{\partial\xi_{-m^{(\rm h)},0}}\Big)=\partial_{\xi}\Big(\frac{1}{\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{+}(\chi^{(\rm n)}_{+}(\eta,\xi_{-m^{(\rm h)},0}),\eta)}\Big)\frac{\partial\chi^{(\rm n)}_{+}}{\partial\xi_{-m^{(\rm h)},0}},\\[5.0pt]
\frac{\partial\chi^{(\rm n)}_{+}}{\partial\xi_{-m^{(\rm h)},0}}(-m^{(\rm h)},\xi_{-m^{(\rm h)},0})=1,\end{array}\right.\end{split}

which implies that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{\partial\chi^{(\rm n)}_{+}}{\partial\xi_{-m^{(\rm h)},0}}=e^{\int^{\eta}_{-m^{(\rm h)}}\partial_{\xi}\big(\frac{1}{\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{+}(\chi^{(\rm n)}_{+}(\tau,\xi_{-m^{(\rm h)},0}),\tau)}\big)d\tau}.\end{split}

By (
4.83
)-(
4.85
), we can obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}\bigg\|\frac{1}{\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{\pm}}-\frac{1}{\underline{\lambda}_{\pm}}\bigg\|_{1,\alpha;\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h)}}&\leq\mathcal{C}\Big(\|\delta z^{(\rm n-1)}\|_{1,\alpha;\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h)}}+\|\delta\tilde{B}^{(\rm h)}_{0}\|_{1,\alpha;\tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm h)}_{\rm in}}+\|\delta\tilde{S}^{(\rm h)}_{0}\|_{1,\alpha;\tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm h)}_{\rm in}}\Big).\end{split}

(4.97)

Then, there exist some constants
\mathcal{C}_{\rm h,3}>0
and
\epsilon_{\rm h,3}>0
depending only on
\tilde{\underline{U}}
and
L
, such that for
\tilde{\epsilon}>0
sufficiently small and

\tilde{\epsilon}<\epsilon_{I}\in(\mathcal{C}_{\rm h,3}\tilde{\epsilon},\epsilon_{\rm h,3})
, we can further get

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{1}{2|\underline{\lambda}_{\pm}|}<\bigg\|\frac{1}{\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{\pm}}\bigg\|_{1,\alpha;\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h)}}<\frac{3}{2|\underline{\lambda}_{\pm}|}.\end{split}

(4.98)

Thus, for
0\leq\eta\leq\ \eta_{\hat{P}}
,
by (
4.98
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\big|\chi^{(\rm n)}_{+}(\eta,\xi_{\hat{P}_{+}(-m^{(\rm h)})})-\chi^{(\rm n)}_{+}(\eta,\xi_{\hat{Q}_{+}(-m^{(\rm h)})})|\leq\mathcal{C}\big|\xi_{\hat{P}_{+}(-m^{(\rm h)})}-\xi_{\hat{Q}_{+}(-m^{(\rm h)})}\big|.\end{split}

(4.99)

On the other hand, we can also deduce from (
4.94
) that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\quad\ \big|\xi_{\hat{P}_{+}(-m^{(\rm h)})}-\xi_{\hat{Q}_{+}(-m^{(\rm h)})}\big|\\[5.0pt]
&\leq\big|\xi_{\hat{P}}-\xi_{\hat{Q}}\big|+\int^{\eta_{\hat{Q}}}_{\eta_{\hat{P}}}\bigg|\frac{1}{\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{+}\big(\hat{Q}_{+}(\tau)\big)}\bigg|d\tau\\[5.0pt]
&\ \ \ \ +\int^{\eta_{\hat{P}}}_{-m^{(\rm h)}}\bigg|\frac{1}{\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{+}\big(\hat{Q}_{+}(\tau)\big)}-\frac{1}{\lambda^{(\rm n-1)}_{+}\big(\hat{P}_{+}(\tau)\big)}\bigg|d\tau\\[5.0pt]
&\leq\mathcal{C}m^{(\rm h)}\bigg(\big\|D\delta z^{(\rm n-1)}\big\|_{0,0;\tilde{\Omega}^{(\rm h)}}+\big\|\big(\delta\tilde{B}^{(\rm h)^{\prime}}_{0},\delta\tilde{S}^{(\rm h)^{\prime}}_{0}\big)\big\|_{0,0;\tilde{\Gamma}^{(\rm h)}_{\rm in}}\bigg)\big|\xi_{\hat{P}_{+}(-m^{(\rm h)})}-\xi_{\hat{Q}_{+}(-m^{(\rm h)})}\big|\\[5.0pt]
&\ \ \ \ +\big|\xi_{\hat{P}}-\xi_{\hat{Q}}\big|+\mathcal{C}\big|\eta_{\hat{P}}-\eta_{\hat{Q}}\big|\\[5.0pt]
